**K39 paper — Verbalized Sampling (arXiv 2510.01171v3, Stanford/Northeastern/WVU, Oct 2025)**:
- Source page: [sources/verbalized-sampling-mode-collapse.md](sources/verbalized-sampling-mode-collapse.md).
- **Core finding**: mode collapse comes from **typicality bias in preference data** (human annotators favor familiar text), not from RLHF algorithm choice. Even with a perfect reward model, RLHF on biased preference data drives collapse.
- **Solution**: training-free **Verbalized Sampling (VS)** prompt pattern — ask the model for a probability distribution over N candidate responses (with `<response>`/`<text>`/`<probability>` tags) instead of a single response. Recovers pretraining-distribution diversity.
- **Numbers**: 1.6-2.1× creative-writing diversity; +6.9 pts downstream math accuracy when used to generate synthetic SFT data (VS-Multi 37.5 vs Direct 30.6); no factuality (§G.7) or safety (§G.8) regression; **stronger models benefit more** (emergent capability-scaling trend).
- **Concept page**: [concepts/verbalized-sampling-llm-diversity.md](concepts/verbalized-sampling-llm-diversity.md) — synthesizes the pattern + 5 Cemini integration vectors (synthetic trading-scenario generation, hypothesis ideation in briefs, conductor reviewer-agent extension to distribution-of-verdicts, Polymarket/Kalshi scenario simulation, news-feed persona simulation).

**Architectural placement** (third orthogonal training-free Conductor layer):

| Layer | When | Mechanism | Source / brief |
|-------|------|-----------|----------------|
| Inference-time generation diversity | At base-agent call site | VS prompt template | **K39 (NEW)** |
| Inference-time call gating | Between propose and execute | Reviewer-agent review (Apple) | K37 reviewer-agent brief (shipped 2026-05-11) |
| Post-hoc trajectory triage | After execution | Non-LLM signals on audit JSONL (DigitalOcean) | K37 signals brief (shipped 2026-05-11) |

All three are training-free, additive, stackable. K39 VS plugs into the base-agent generation call site; the reviewer-agent brief already in prod can be extended (v2) to request a distribution of verdicts instead of binary approve/revise.

## [2026-05-10] adoption | skill_audit v0.5 ship — codex pressure-test 4-mode adversarial review + `_llm_retry.py` Phase 2 ported

Brief-audit upgrade sprint **step 3 SHIPPED 2026-05-10** as `scripts/skill_audit.py` v0.5 (opt-in `--llm-mode` flag) + new `scripts/_llm_retry.py` (3-tier structured-output retry helper). Closes the K32 batch's `codex-startup-pressure-test-skill` STEAL-FROM action AND `obsidian-llm-wiki-local` Phase 2 in a single commit — the two adoptions were always entangled (one provides the prompts, the other the retry plumbing).

- **Four codex modes re-styled for brief-audit context** (was: startup-idea evaluation; now: tool-adoption-brief evaluation):
  - `pressure-test` — find fatal flaws in adopting this tool
  - `problem-validation` — is the Cemini pain this brief addresses real, or imagined?
  - `competition-map` — does Cemini's existing stack already cover this? (kept discipline that current behavior is always a competitor)
  - `mvp-plan` — what is the smallest test that proves/kills the adoption?
  - NOT ported: `first-10-customers` (irrelevant — we're not selling to customers), `full` (redundant with combined-call architecture).
- **Architecture: single combined-call structured output**. One DeepSeek `deepseek-chat` API call per brief returning a Pydantic v2 schema (`FatalFlaw[]` + `verdict` + `competition_check` + `summary` + `model` + `latency_ms`). Aligns with olw's small-flat-schema discipline; keeps API cost to 1 call per `--llm-mode` invocation.
- **Advisory by default**: dynamic findings render alongside static checks but do NOT change the static score. Static veto gates (regime-disclosure, Operational Stability, Scientific Integrity) take precedence. `--strict-dynamic` flag upgrades a dynamic "reject" verdict to top-level REJECT iff no static veto gates already fired.
- **NEW: `scripts/_llm_retry.py`** (~280 lines, Phase 2 port of `kytmanov/obsidian-llm-wiki-local/src/obsidian_llm_wiki/structured_output.py`). 3-tier retry:
  - Tier 1: DeepSeek `response_format={"type":"json_object"}` + fill-in-template system prompt + `_unwrap` post-processor (handles 3 common mis-wrappings: single-key dict, single-key string-encoded JSON, JSON-Schema echo with `properties` wrapper).
  - Tier 2: regex `_extract_json`: ` ```json` fence → bare ` ``` ` → first `{...}`.
  - Tier 3: re-prompt with parser error embedded, up to `max_retries=2`.
  - Ollama-specific bits stripped; telemetry reduced to single stderr log line per call (`[_llm_retry] stage=... model=... tier=... retries=... latency_ms=... status=ok|fail`).
  - Module defaults: `DEFAULT_MODEL="deepseek-chat"`, `DEFAULT_BASE_URL="https://api.deepseek.com/v1"`. `_get_client()` builds `openai.OpenAI(api_key=$DEEPSEEK_API_KEY)` lazily.
- **Calibration result** (`briefs/2026-05-10_aris-adoption.md`):
  - Tier 1 success in 14805ms on first try (no retries triggered)
  - Dynamic verdict: **REWORK** (signal-bearing — static checks had passed at 99.5/100; dynamic check caught the strategic flaw the static checks couldn't see)
  - 4 fatal flaws surfaced (one per mode):
    - [HIGH/pressure-test] Triage of 78 SKILL.md files deferred without methodology — fast test: "Pick 3 skills (idea-creator, experiment-plan, research-wiki) and benchmark each against existing wiki/Conductor workflows within 1 day."
    - [HIGH/problem-validation] Existing pipelines not demonstrated to fail; pain point unvalidated — fast test: "Document one recent OSINT task where current tooling fell short and ARIS would have helped."
    - [MEDIUM/competition-map] ARIS duplicates DeepSeek + wiki scripts + LangGraph + kb-server — fast test: "List 3 specific tasks ARIS could do that current Cemini stack cannot."
    - [MEDIUM/mvp-plan] Phase 1 plan vague — fast test: "Copy idea-creator/SKILL.md to ~/.claude/skills/ and invoke it on SSRN 4943355 within 1 day; pass if output materially exceeds current wiki idea-page format."
  - Competition check correctly named Cemini's existing stack: "DeepSeek for synthesis, wiki scripts for structured output, LangGraph for multi-step workflows, kb-server for knowledge management"
  - Telemetry: `[_llm_retry] stage=skill_audit_dynamic model=deepseek-chat tier=1 retries=0 latency_ms=14805 status=ok`
- **Updated entities**:
  - `wiki/entities/tools/codex-startup-pressure-test-skill.md` → `maturity: validated`, new `adopted_as: scripts/skill_audit.py v0.5 (2026-05-10)` field, `adopted` tag added, full adoption-status block replacing pending-Phase-1 placeholder.
  - `wiki/entities/tools/obsidian-llm-wiki-local.md` → `maturity: validated`, new `phase2_ported_as: scripts/_llm_retry.py (2026-05-10)` field, `partial-adopted` tag added. Phase 1 (Phase-0 audit) + Phase 2 (3-tier retry port) both DONE; Phases 3-4 (orphan suggestion + bidirectional wikilink auto-rewrite in `wiki_lint.py`) deferred — pain-driven, no current need.
- **Sprint COMPLETE for the K32 batch**: 4 patterns ported into `skill_audit.py` across v0.3 / v0.4 / v0.5. Hierarchical section optimization (3rd DR-named research-paper pattern) deferred — can plug into the same `--llm-mode` infrastructure when a real need arises.
- **Anti-pattern avoided** — build-on-demand discipline: the decision to defer `_llm_retry.py` from 2026-05-06 to 2026-05-10 was correct. The helper's API shape (e.g., `stage=` parameter for telemetry tagging, `api_key=` override for tests) was driven by `skill_audit.py`'s actual needs, not invented preemptively.

**Updated**: `scripts/skill_audit.py` (v0.4 → v0.5), `scripts/_llm_retry.py` (NEW), `wiki/entities/tools/codex-startup-pressure-test-skill.md`, `wiki/entities/tools/obsidian-llm-wiki-local.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `hot.md`.

## [2026-05-10] adoption | skill_audit v0.4 ship — research-paper-writing claim-evidence + abstract synthesis ported

Brief-audit upgrade sprint **step 2 SHIPPED 2026-05-10** as `scripts/skill_audit.py` v0.4. Two new static checks distilled from `master-cai/Research-Paper-Writing-Skills` (K32 STEAL-FROM brief). Pattern source is abandoned (4 commits / 0 releases) — re-styled as deterministic Python checks rather than DeepSeek prompts; LLM-as-judge dynamic-check version deferred to upcoming codex pressure-test step.

- **`check_claim_evidence`** (weight 0.06) — adoption-brief claim-evidence alignment. Detects 4 claim labels (license `MIT`/`Apache-2.0`/`AGPL-3.0`/..., version `vX.Y.Z`, stars `N★`/`N stars`, commits `N commits`). Each must co-locate with a verification anchor within ±240 chars (`VERIFIED YYYY-MM-DD`, `Phase-0 audit`, `audit ✓`, `[Source: ...]`, `[NEEDS VERIFICATION ...]`, `[CONFIRMED]`/`[TENTATIVE]`/`[RETRACTED]`). Duplicate claims deduped via canonical-value grouping; claim anchored if ANY occurrence has anchor nearby. Coverage thresholds: ≥90% full credit (0.06), ≥70% (0.04), ≥50% (0.02), <50% (0.0).
- **`check_abstract_synthesis`** (weight 0.04) — `## Summary` section quality. Three signals: (a) length 50-400 words, (b) effort estimate present, (c) verdict-flavored opener (`Adopt`/`Steal-from`/`Reject`/`Reference`/`Defer`/`Install`/`GO`/`NO-GO`/`CONDITIONAL-GO`). 3/3 full credit (0.04), 2/3 (0.025), 1/3 (0.01), 0/3 (0.0).
- **Weight rebalance**: 0.10 of headroom carved out of `check_citations` (0.15 → 0.10) and `check_prose_quality` (0.10 → 0.05). v0.4 sums to 1.00 across 12 static checks.
- **Adoption-class gate**: both new checks exempt ops/research briefs via `_is_adoption_brief()` — same gate already used by v0.3's `check_phasing`/`check_risk_discipline`/`check_brief_completeness`.
- **Calibration verified** against 7 representative briefs:
  - K35 fresh adoption (aris, spec-kit, codeboarding, tech-debt): 99.5/100 PASS. Initial ARIS run WARN 98.5 due to verdict-line regex (asterisks wrapping extra text broke `\*\*GO\*\*` match); fixed by tightening verdict to `**GO** — selective adoption deferred 2026-05-10.`
  - Anchoring gaps caught: claude-mem 97.5 WARN (un-anchored `v13.0.1` mention outside Phase-0 context), revpdf 94.5 WARN (`Proprietary (free)` not co-located with verification).
  - Older adoption briefs flag legitimate gaps: hftbacktest 90.5, polymarket-stack 90.5, picobot-prod 96.0, adala-prod 98.5, guidance-ai 94.5.
  - Ops/research briefs properly exempt: step81-sshd 85.0 N/A, polymarket-onchain 100/100 N/A. Check is signal-bearing, not noise.
- **Anti-pattern fix during calibration**: initial `check_claim_evidence` over-counted 4 MIT mentions in one brief as 4 separate claims. ## [2026-05-08] training | RL 80K-frame BTC-USD MAPPO run completed

Completed 80,384-frame MAPPO training run on BTC-USD with 33 days of historical data, achieving best Differential Sortino Ratio (DSoR) of 0.1059. Generated ensemble inference placeholder checkpoint for future 500K-frame retrain when sufficient data becomes available (~October 2026).

### Training Overview
- **Algorithm**: Multi-Agent PPO (MAPPO) with 5 specialized agents + centralized Critic
- **Observation Space**: 41-dimensional feature set  
- **Reward Function**: Differential Sortino Ratio (DSoR)
- **Data Limitations**: Only 33 days usable data (vs 500K-frame target requiring ~174 days); empty sources vol_surface, sector_rotation, sentiment logs defaulted to 0.0
- **Workaround**: Used container IP (172.23.0.8) directly due to Docker DNS resolution failure for 'postgres' host

### Results
- **Best DSoR**: 0.10586416970845691 (~0.1059)
- **Frames Collected**: 80,384
- **Training Time**: 179.23 seconds (~3 minutes)
- **Total Parameters**: 71,384

### Checkpoint Artifacts
- `checkpoint_80k_btc_20260508_final.json` - training metadata
- `checkpoint_80k_btc_20260508_final.safetensors` - model weights

## [2026-05-07] ingest | K33 — BLADE listwise alignment + Regime-Conditioned BO evaluation

Two arXiv PDFs ingested from `research to be indexed/`:
1. `Beyond Static Best-of-N- Bayesian List-wise Alignment.pdf` (arXiv 2605.04559, USTC + Zhejiang U, CC BY 4.0, SIGIR '26)
2. `Regime-Conditioned Evaluation in Multi-Context Bayesian Optimization.pdf` (arXiv 2605.04895, MBZUAI, Noel Thomas)

### Action-value ordering

Paper 2 (Regime-Conditioned BO) is the **highest action-value paper of K33**. It directly upgrades brief-audit gates with a `regime-disclosure` requirement (1–2 hr port priority), Conductor fitness function discipline, and strategy backtest evaluation. Paper 1 (BLADE) is methodological reference for ranking-task implementations downstream of this.

### Pages created (5)

- `wiki/sources/blade-bayesian-listwise-alignment.md` — BLADE source page. Two failure modes diagnosed (Indiscriminate Supervision + Gradient Decay under vBoN N→large) + Bayesian fix (prior over latent permutations × likelihood scaled by relevance gap). NDCG@5 0.0410 vs BoN-Alignment 0.0333. Verdict: **STUDY-METHODOLOGY-only** (companion repo unlicensed).
- `wiki/sources/regime-conditioned-bo-evaluation.md` — Noel Thomas source page. **No-Free-Leaderboard proposition** (when CATE flips sign across regime, ATE = free parameter of regime mixture). PRS = (B/|A|)(1−ρ) Portable Regime Score. REGIMEPLANNER online-switching adaptive planner. GDSC2 cancer-screening reversal case study.
- `wiki/concepts/llm-listwise-alignment.md` — Concept hub for BLADE methodology applied to 5 Cemini surfaces (ticker watchlist re-ranking, news/source ranking, Conductor routing, brief screening, strategy ensemble selection).
- `wiki/concepts/regime-conditioned-evaluation.md` — Concept hub for PRS + No-Free-Leaderboard + REGIMEPLANNER. Three operational targets: brief-audit `regime-disclosure` gate, Conductor fitness function, strategy backtest discipline. Minimum reporting standard: per-regime CATE table + ρ + B/|A| + PRS, not aggregate-only.
- `wiki/entities/tools/blade-github.md` — STUDY-METHODOLOGY-ONLY verdict for `RegionCh/BLADE` repo. Snapshot 2026-05-07: 1 star, 0 forks, last push 2026-01-27 (3+ months pre-publication), no LICENSE file → default copyright. Mode: `cemini-license-posture` "Unknown / no LICENSE." Re-implement from CC BY 4.0 paper itself; do NOT vendor.

- API-backed commands (`rewind`, `rewind commit`, `rewind ask`) require key configuration via env vars or `rewind config set <provider>` — deferred to user choice between DeepSeek (reuse Cemini key), Groq (free-tier fast), Gemini (large-context-free-tier), or local Ollama (privacy-via-tunnel).
- Cemini-prod-side: NOT applicable. git-rewind is a developer-workflow tool, not a production component.

## [2026-05-05] audit | Claude-OSINT Phase 0 — VERDICT: NO-PORT (ROADMAP #15)

Cloned `https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-OSINT` (MIT, 476★, last commit 2026-04-27). K30 eval claim accurate on lines (~5,861) and license, but framing was misleading: not "thousands of small skills" — only **2 large skills** (osint-methodology 1,693 lines + offensive-osint 4,168 lines).

Phase 0 categorization across both skills:
- 0 PORT-DIRECTLY
- 0 PORT-WITH-ADAPTATION
- **2 DUPLICATE-DOMAIN** (both target external reconnaissance / red-team OSINT — subdomain enumeration, SSO/SAML mapping, attack-surface, secret scanning, threat-actor investigation)
- 0 OFF-DOMAIN
- 0 DEFER

**Domain mismatch, not technical mismatch.** Format is directly compatible (Anthropic's official Claude Code skill format with YAML frontmatter + triggers); skills would load into `~/.claude/skills/` without modification. But "compatible" ≠ "useful": Claude-OSINT helps a user *conduct reconnaissance* (find target's subdomains, validate target's credentials, identify target's attack paths). Cemini's curator workflow helps *ingest research sources* (validate provenance, extract takeaways, cross-link concepts, build indexes). Orthogonal problem spaces.

Concrete failure mode: if a Cemini session pulled `offensive-osint` on the trigger keyword "secret scanning," Claude would receive 4,168 lines on finding leaked AWS keys in a target's GitHub repos — not on how to validate a source that *discusses* AWS secret-management. Skill fires on keyword, dumps irrelevant context.

The selectivity hard rule (codified in `@concepts/curator-skills.md` 2026-05-05): "if Cemini's K-series ingest discipline already covers a skill's domain, don't merge — duplicate coverage adds noise." Cemini already covers source-side validation via preingest_check.py + skill_audit.py + wiki_gap_detect.py + cross_doc_dedup.py. Both Claude-OSINT skills fall in DUPLICATE-DOMAIN. Reject.

### Eval-quality lesson logged

The K30 Gemini DR eval used phrasing "5,500 lines of specialized SKILL.md files" (plural) which misled granularity expectation. Real shape: 2 large reference-document-style skills, not many small tactical skills. **Lesson for future Gemini DR sweeps**: when evaluating skill libraries, ask explicitly "how many distinct skills" alongside "how many lines." Documented in `@entities/tools/claude-osint.md` "Eval-quality footnote" section for K-series ingest discipline.

### Activation triggers (re-evaluate if either fires)

- Cemini needs OSINT-style external-reconnaissance capabilities (e.g., counterparty tech-stack risk assessment, competitor monitoring via subdomain enumeration)
- A future Claude-OSINT version adds curator-workflow skills (source ingestion, citation management, wiki page generation)

Until then, shelved.

### `curator-skills.md` page stays empty

`@concepts/curator-skills.md` was created as a stub accumulator (K30 #24). It now records the NO-PORT verdict + watch list of future-candidate skill sources (claude-obsidian, kepano-obsidian-skills, XSKILL paper's framework). The discipline rule stays in force: any future skill merged into `~/.claude/skills/` requires an entry on this page.

After K28 SwarmVault NO-GO + K30 PageIndex NO-GO + K30 Claude-OSINT NO-PORT, the workspace's audit pattern has shipped a clean record of three "credible-looking but wrong-fit" tool rejections in two sessions. ## [2026-05-05] decide | atlas-gic + XSKILL → audit-trail subset (codex-reviewed scope reduction)

User asked for codex-agent independent review of the K28 atlas-gic + K29 XSKILL Conductor-evolution briefs before committing to GO/NO-GO on the operational-cost commitment. Codex review surfaced three correctness issues:

1. **Per-cycle LLM cost was 2-7× understated**: XSKILL's `propose_via_critique()` runs per-taxonomy-class (8+ classes) not once per cycle. Plus skill_writer.py adds judgement-gate calls per proposed skill. Revised estimate $0.50-1.80/cycle, not $0.10-0.25.

2. **The "≤25% of weekly Conductor budget" cap is degenerate at current traffic**: at ~10 decisions/day, 25% = $0.026/week which is below even the low-end cost estimate. Cap as written would prohibit running the system.

3. **The ≥5% fitness-improvement gate is statistically undetectable**: Cohen's d 0.2 with n=30 → power <50%. Reliably detecting 5% needs ~300+ shadow decisions per evaluation = ~300 days at current 10-decisions/day rate.

Codex's load-bearing finding: the IP-sale "demonstrable self-improvement capability" audit-trail story is achievable from `routing.jsonl` + git-tracked config history + structured audit JSONL **without the LLM mutation engine**. The remaining 15% (automated proposal) is what requires the autoresearch loop, which isn't justified at current scale.

### Decision: scope-reduce both briefs to audit-trail subset

New superseding brief: `briefs/2026-05-05_conductor-audit-trail-phase1.md` (14.4 KB, scp'd to prod).

- Implements **only Phases 1 + 5** of the original autoresearch brief: fitness function (with per-class breakdown to surface class-level regressions early) + structured audit JSONL + git-tracked config commits with structured commit messages
- **No LLM calls; no cron; no auto-promotion** — manual or post-incident `--build-priors` triggers only
- ~2-4 days prod-side engineering, ~$0/week ongoing operational cost
- Captures ~85% of the IP-sale narrative value at ~2% of the engineering cost (per codex)

### Two predecessor briefs kept staged but marked DEFERRED

- `briefs/2026-05-04_autoresearch-conductor-evolution.md` — Phases 2-6 deferred. Top-of-file STATUS header added pointing to the superseding brief. Re-scp'd to prod.
- `briefs/2026-05-04_xskill-cross-rollout-critique-conductor.md` — entirely deferred. Top-of-file STATUS header added. Re-scp'd to prod.

Both briefs retained intact (do NOT delete or modify the deferred phases) for future activation when **both** triggers fire:
1. Traffic threshold: ≥ 3,500 decisions/week sustained for ≥ 2 weeks
2. Demonstrated value pull: explicit buyer/research signal for the "automated self-proposing" differentiator

### ROADMAP

- Item #6 (atlas-gic): partially superseded; struck-through with deferred-phases note
- Item #6b (XSKILL): entirely deferred; struck-through with deferred-status note
- Item #6c (NEW): "Conductor audit-trail Phase 1+5" — the subset that ships now

### Methodology note

The codex review pattern worked well: independent agent reading both briefs catches statistical and cost-cap errors that the original drafter (me) didn't surface. Logging this for future curator-discipline reuse: when a brief's GO/NO-GO has substantial commitments (engineering days + operational cost + statistical claims), dispatch codex/Plan/code-reviewer for an independent pass before the user signs off. Saves wrong-direction implementation effort.

## [2026-05-05] audit | PageIndex Phase 0 — VERDICT: NO-GO (ROADMAP #11)

Cloned `https://github.com/VectifyAI/PageIndex` to `/tmp/pageindex-audit/` for Phase 0 source audit (similar template to K28 SwarmVault audit). Eval claim accurate: MIT licensed, vectorless reasoning-based RAG. Eight load-bearing questions answered.

Four disqualifying findings, any one alone sufficient:

1. **Q1 single-file API**: PageIndex's `PageIndexClient.index(file_path)` and the underlying `page_index()` accept ONE file at a time. No directory walking / glob / batch API. Cemini's 530+ wiki pages + 99 nested entity files would require 600+ separate index instances + a registry layer mapping queries to instances. kb-server handles all of this with one flat index.

2. **Q2 agentic cost per query**: PageIndex uses LLM-driven reasoning at query time (agent uses `get_document_structure()` to walk the index tree, then auto-calls `get_page_content(pages="N-M")`). 2-4 LLM calls per query → ~$0.001-0.005/query at GPT-4o mini rates, multi-second latency. kb-server is sub-100ms FTS at $0/query. At Cemini's expected wiki query volume, the operational economics flip.

3. **Q5 + Q6 lost graph + lost metadata**: PageIndex doesn't parse `@path/to/page.md` cross-links (Cemini has 3,400+) or frontmatter metadata (`type`, `tags`, `keywords`, `related[]`, `maturity`). Both would be indexed as plain text — wiki graph topology and structured-filter capability lost.

4. **Q8 use-case mismatch**: PageIndex is built for long-form professional document QA (financial PDFs, legal docs, multi-hundred-page research papers). Cemini's wiki articles average 2-10 KB / 100-500 tokens — exactly the size class where FTS wins and reasoning-based retrieval is overkill.

NOT generically rejected — rejected for *Cemini's wiki layer specifically*. Re-evaluation triggers documented inline in `entities/tools/pageindex.md`: PageIndex becomes a candidate ONLY IF Cemini begins indexing long-form professional documents as a separate retrieval tier (e.g., SEC EDGAR 10-K/10-Q filings, CFTC ANPRM filings, the research-corpus PDFs in `/opt/cemini-bulk/research/`).

Files touched:
- `wiki/entities/tools/pageindex.md` (draft → validated; Phase 0 verdict + 8-question table + re-evaluation triggers + audit-path-deferred section)
- `wiki/concepts/wiki-tooling-evaluation.md` candidate landscape table (PageIndex marked NO-GO; Deep GraphRAG remains the only librarian-tooling alternative on file)
- ROADMAP item #11 marked DONE-NO-GO

After K28 SwarmVault NO-GO + this PageIndex NO-GO, the Karpathy-pattern alternative-to-kb-server search has eliminated 2 of 3 K-series candidates. **Default-stay-on-kb-server is the operational answer** unless Deep GraphRAG triggers a re-evaluation by the conditions above.

## [2026-05-04] ingest | XSKILL paper + LeetCode prep stub (K29)

User dropped 2 sources: `XSKILL- Continual Learning from Experience and Skills in Multimodal Agents.pdf` (arXiv 2603.12056, HKUST + Zhejiang U + HUST, 27 pp) + `60 Popular LeetCode Problems & Solution for SDET.pdf` (off-domain personal reference). Pre-ingest dedup: 2 NEW.

### XSKILL — substantive on-domain ingest

Dual-stream multimodal-agent learning architecture:
- **Skills** (Markdown library, task-level workflows + tool templates)
- **Experiences** (JSON bank, action-level decision-making insights)
- **MLLMexec / MLLMkb privilege separation** (different model for execution vs knowledge management — implements MITRE ATLAS M0032)
- **Cross-rollout critique** (Phase I): multi-rollouts → MLLM-judged comparison → hierarchical consolidation through length/similarity/judgement filters
- **Subtask decomposition + top-k retrieval + visual adaptation** (Phase II): inject adapted skills + experiences into agent system prompt at inference

Empirical: +2.58 to +6.71 Avg@4 across 5 benchmarks (VisualToolBench, TIR-Bench, MMSearch-Plus, MMBrowseComp, AgentVista) × 4 backbones (Gemini-2.5-Pro, Gemini-3-Flash, GPT-5-mini, o4-mini); +11.13pp on TIR-Bench vs Agent-KB. Cross-model transferability demonstrated.

**Cemini fit (high)**: XSKILL's architecture maps onto Cemini's existing infrastructure with surprising fidelity:
- Skill Library (Markdown) ⇔ `wiki/concepts/` Markdown procedure pages
- Experience Bank (JSON) ⇔ stash episodic memory layer (Postgres+pgvector)
- MLLMexec/MLLMkb separation ⇔ DeepSeek (analyst) + future heavier model for knowledge mgmt
- Cross-rollout critique ⇔ atlas-gic clean-room re-implementation (already briefed at `briefs/2026-05-04_autoresearch-conductor-evolution.md`)
- Subtask decomposition + top-k retrieval ⇔ kb-server REST API + stash recall MCP

**Most actionable take-away**: XSKILL's cross-rollout critique mechanism is the *complementary* missing piece to atlas-gic's git-commit/revert loop. Combine: Conductor's routing-decision logs → MLLM-critiqued (XSKILL-style) → refined routing rules emit as git commits (atlas-gic-style).

The Gemini DR eval treated tennis-prediction and medical-research as out-of-domain ("orthogonal to financial research") — that verdict is wrong. tennis_match_prediction maps directly to tennis prediction-market modeling; medical-research-skills map to FDA-approval prediction-market and healthcare-equity research alike (per user note 2026-05-04: medical skills are useful for healthcare equity trading too — biotechs, pharma, drug-trial-driven catalysts).

The llm-wiki-compiler v0.4.0 deployment-block (hardcoded `CONCEPTS_DIR`) has been outstanding since 2026-04-29 with kb-server as workaround. 5. **Conductor evolution candidate**: atlas-gic's autoresearch loop is the architectural blueprint for evolving Conductor v0.1.1 from human-tuned routing (`--build-priors` manually triggered) to autoresearch-driven routing (proposal → shadow-mode evaluation → automated promote-or-revert). ## [2026-05-04] persist | laptop stash tunnel under launchd (KeepAlive verified)

Replaced bare `ssh -f -N -L 8088:localhost:8080 cemini-librarian` (this morning's wiring) with a launchd plist at `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cemini.stash-tunnel.plist`. Pivoted from autossh because Homebrew isn't installed on the laptop and launchd is the macOS-native zero-dep path.

### Plist behavior
- `RunAtLoad: true` — tunnel comes up at login (no manual `ssh` needed after laptop boot/wake-from-cold).
- `KeepAlive: true` + `ThrottleInterval: 10` — respawn ssh within 10s of any drop (network change, server reboot, idle timeout).
- `ServerAliveInterval=30` + `ServerAliveCountMax=3` + `ExitOnForwardFailure=yes` — exit cleanly if the SSH session goes silent or the local-port-forward fails to bind, so launchd can restart instead of holding a half-broken tunnel.

### Verification
Killed the running ssh PID; observed launchd respawn within ~10s with new PID. SSE handshake (`curl http://localhost:8088/sse`) returned HTTP 200 against the new PID. Pre-/post-state logged in `~/Library/Logs/cemini-stash-tunnel.log` (currently empty — success path produces no stderr).

### Pages updated
- `concepts/cemini-deployment.md` §9 persistence — replaced "launchd plist (TBD)" placeholder with the full plist XML + load/unload commands. autossh kept as alternative-if-brew-already-installed.

### Open
- Apply the same launchd-plist pattern when wiring kb-server or other librarian-side MCPs to the laptop (would need parallel plist with different Label + port mapping).

## [2026-05-04] wire | stash + brave-search MCP across all 3 Claude Code contexts (laptop + librarian + prod)

Extended the morning's laptop-only stash wiring to cover all three contexts the user actually works in. Same MCP toolkit, different connection strings driven by network topology.

### What changed (server-side)
- **`cemini-librarian:/root/.claude.json`** — added `stash → http://localhost:8080/sse` (no tunnel — stash is local) + `brave-search` (stdio, npx, same API key as laptop). Backup at `/root/.claude.json.bak-2026-05-04`.
- **`cemini-prod:/root/.claude.json`** — added `stash → http://10.0.0.2:8080/sse` (private Hetzner network, no tunnel — prod is `10.0.0.3`, librarian is `10.0.0.2`) + `brave-search` (same as librarian). Backup at `/root/.claude.json.bak-2026-05-04`.
- **`/opt/cemini/.mcp.json`** (prod, project-level) — already had `conductor` wired at project level since 2026-05-02. No change needed.

### Verification
Three-context SSE handshake — all return HTTP 200:
```
Laptop  → tunnel:8088 → librarian:8080  : HTTP 200 (curl times out at 3s, expected for SSE)
Librarian → localhost:8080               : HTTP 200
Prod    → 10.0.0.2:8080 (private net)    : HTTP 200
```

### Why no tunnel on the servers
Stash binds to `*:8080` and `*:9090` (verified via `ss -tlnp`). The Hetzner Cloud Firewall blocks the *public* edge, but the private `10.0.0.0/24` network bypasses the firewall — prod can hit librarian's `10.0.0.2:8080` directly. Only the laptop, sitting outside Hetzner, needs the SSH local-port-forward.

### Pages updated
- `concepts/cemini-deployment.md` §9a — new sub-section with the multi-context wiring matrix (laptop tunnel vs librarian-localhost vs prod-private-network)
- `entities/infrastructure/cemini-stack.md` — stash bullet expanded to document the three-context wiring landed today
- `log.md` — this entry

### Activation gates (all three)
Each Claude Code context picks up `~/.claude.json` only at process start. Restart in each context to activate. The user noted active sessions running, so activation is deferred until natural restart of each.

## [2026-05-04] wire | stash MCP — laptop Claude Code integration

Wired stash episodic-memory MCP into the laptop's Claude Code session. Stash itself was deployed on `cemini-librarian` 2026-05-02; the laptop link landed today.

### What changed
- **SSH local-port-forward**: `ssh -f -N -L 8088:localhost:8080 -o ServerAliveInterval=60 -o ExitOnForwardFailure=yes cemini-librarian` — laptop:8088 → librarian:localhost:8080 (stash SSE).
- **SSE handshake verified**: `curl -sv -H "Accept: text/event-stream" --max-time 3 http://localhost:8088/sse | head -10` returns `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` + `Content-Type: text/event-stream`.
- **`~/.claude.json` mcpServers entry**: added `stash → {type: sse, url: http://localhost:8088/sse}` via Python (file too large for inline edit; backup at `~/.claude.json.bak-2026-05-04`).
- **Activation gate**: requires Claude Code restart to load the new MCP server.

### Why a tunnel (not direct connection)
Stash ports 8080/9090 are blocked at the Hetzner Cloud Firewall — TCP timeout from laptop, immediate response from server-local. The SSH tunnel is the only valid public-side path. Documented as the canonical integration pattern in `concepts/cemini-deployment.md` §9.

- `entities/infrastructure/cemini-stack.md` — stash bullet now notes laptop wiring landed 2026-05-04 + the firewall rationale.

The three link-evaluation docx files are Cemini-internal briefs authored 2026-05-01; together they evaluate **64 software projects** against the standard three-surface framework. This is the largest single-day link-evaluation batch in the K-series and the first Wave authored directly as `.docx` link-eval briefs rather than Gemini Deep Research outputs.

### Source pages created (4)

- `wiki/sources/wave-5-1-link-eval-stacks.md` — 25 URLs (systems-architecture focus). 3 Adopt (DeepSeek-V4-Pro Fireworks, Hyper-Extract, TimesFM), 6 Steal-from, 5 Reference, 6 Reject. AGPL-3.0 pattern: FinceptTerminal + ST3GG + MiroFish all hard-rejected on license alone.
- `wiki/sources/wave-5-1-link-eval-more.md` — 12 URLs (agent-framework focus). 2 Adopt (Instructor, DSPy), 1 Steal-from (crewAI yaml schemas), 2 Defer, 3 Reference, 4 Reject. Central architectural thesis: LangGraph's linear 4-node StateGraph is the non-negotiable foundation — competing orchestrators are categorically rejected.
- `wiki/sources/wave-5-1-link-eval-even-more.md` — 27 URLs (infra + data-engineering focus). 4 Adopt (files-to-prompt, crawl4ai, trafilatura, unstructured forming a coherent 4-stage extraction pipeline), 2 Steal-from, 5 Reference, 16 Reject. Highest Adopt count of the three; extraction-pipeline architecture is the most concrete operational deliverable.
- `wiki/sources/situational-awareness-aschenbrenner.md` — Leopold Aschenbrenner's Situational Awareness (June 2024, 165pp). Skimmed (ToC + first 10pp); full deep-read pending. AGI-by-2027 thesis via OOM counting; intelligence explosion; trillion-dollar cluster; superalignment; The Project.

### Entity pages created (3)

- `wiki/entities/models/timesfm.md` — Google Research 200M-param time-series foundation model (Apache-2.0). CPU-viable (~400-800 MB). Complementary to Chronos-Bolt (8M fast-path vs 200M deep-forecast).
- `wiki/entities/frameworks/dspy.md` — Stanford prompt-programming framework (Apache-2.0). Offline compilation pattern: GEPA-optimize prompts on laptop, ship static strings to production LangGraph nodes — no DSPy runtime on Hetzner.
- `wiki/entities/tools/hermes-lcm.md` — Lossless Context Management engine (MIT). DAG-based hierarchical summarization extending the cpr-pattern with L1/L2/L3 escalation tiers + lossless pointers. Steal-from extraction target: reimplement DAG model + lcm_grep/lcm_expand in LangGraph/PostgreSQL.

### Concept pages updated (3)

- `concepts/context-compression-resume-pattern.md` — added backlinks to hermes-lcm + wave-5-1-link-eval-stacks
- `concepts/wiki-tooling-evaluation.md` — added backlinks to all three Wave 5.1 link-eval source pages
- `concepts/mcp-tool-marketplace.md` — added backlink to wave-5-1-link-eval-stacks

### Cross-Wave patterns surfaced

1. **LangGraph firewall** — Competing agent orchestrators are categorically rejected regardless of technical merit. This architectural boundary is now explicit and non-negotiable.
2. **AGPL-3.0 poison-pill accumulation** — 5 additional AGPL-3.0 rejections across the three docs (Unsloth, ST3GG, MiroFish, FinceptTerminal, OASIS-transitive). The $50K/yr/organization documented penalty on FinceptTerminal is the most severe license exposure in any Wave evaluation.
3. **Extraction-pipeline composition** — First Wave where four Adopt-tier tools compose into a coherent pipeline (crawl4ai → trafilatura → unstructured → files-to-prompt).
4. **Offline compilation pattern** — DSPy + Instructor together define a new architectural pattern: optimize on laptop, ship static artifacts to production. This preserves IP-sale cleanliness and avoids runtime dependency bloat.

## [2026-04-30] deep-read | K10 — 3_26 b.docx: Cemini Internal Agent Architecture Audit (5 Q&A)

Deep-read 5,541-word Cemini-internal solo-developer architecture audit (~2026-03-26). Five Q&A sections: mobile HITL (LangGraph interrupt/Command(resume) + Redis Streams → OIDC/CIBA upgrade path), agent reliability (MAST Compound Reliability Decay, max 5-step chains, Pydantic validation nodes, fallback to heuristics NOT smaller LLMs), financial MCP servers (FMP SOC 2 primary, AlphaVantage MIT, Octagon AI, SEC EDGAR MCP), local DeepSeek-R1-14B CPU viability (8.8-11.3 t/s Q5_K_M, Docker cgroups mandatory, NUMA single-socket > dual-socket), hybrid cloud/local Supervisor routing (PostgreSQL Shared Scratchpad, 30-70% cost reduction claimed). Three actionable findings (OIDC 4-5d, cgroups 4-6h, circuit breakers 2-3d). Three blind spots (checkpoint state bloat, sandbox security, TorchRL↔LLM latency sync). Prescribed-vs-deployed gap: PostgresSaver not wired (K8), local LLM retracted, circuit breakers not found, mobile HITL unknown.

## [2026-04-30] deep-read | Piebald-AI/claude-code-system-prompts — 8 structural patterns cataloged

Deep-read via GitHub API directory enumeration → `curl` fetch of 14 representative prompt fragment files. Promoted `read-skim` → `deep-read`. 8 transferable structural patterns cataloged for Cemini's FastMCP tool decorators:

1. **File format**: HTML comment header (`name`, `description`, `ccVersion`, `variables`) → prompt body
2. **Variable templating**: `${CONSTANT}` (static) vs `${FUNCTION()}` (runtime) — maps to FastMCP `description_template` with same syntax
3. **4-section tool description**: imperative one-liner → Usage → When to Use/When NOT to Use → Tips (the disambiguation section is the highest-value structural lift)
4. **Anti-patterns**: explicit "do not" constraints in system prompts — maps to `anti_patterns:` field on FastMCP decorators
5. **Communication discipline**: one sentence before first tool call, one per update, 1-2 sentence end-of-turn summary — maps to trading-agent silence budget
6. **Scope containment**: "don't implement until user agrees" / "a bug fix doesn't need surrounding cleanup" — maps to position-check-not-execute for trading agents
7. **Confirmation gate**: uncertain operations gate behind user confirmation — maps to risk-manager trade-confirmation gate
8. **Context isolation**: sub-agent prompts as context-containment boundaries — maps to analyst/researcher/trader/risk-manager role-specific prompt fragments

Three highest-ROI structural lifts identified (~8 hours): tool disambiguation (Pattern 3), `anti_patterns:` field (Pattern 4), context isolation audit (Pattern 8).

### Pages updated
- `wiki/sources/piebald-claude-code-system-prompts.md` — read_status promoted; Structural Pattern Catalog section added; adoption assessment updated with concrete implementation steps
- `wiki/index.md` — entry updated to reflect deep-read + 8-pattern catalog

## [2026-04-30] concept | MCP Tool Marketplace synthesis — Cemini's MCP-server adoption pattern

After 4 waves of Gemini DR ingests (Waves 2/3/4) the wiki had accumulated 13+ MCP-server source pages and 5+ Claude-Code-skill source pages without cross-cutting synthesis. Each new MCP-server evaluation was triggering a fresh "wait, is this like vibe-trading or like dune-analytics-mcp?" comparison loop. Today's session shipped 11 dedicated source pages alone (Wave 4 phase 1 + 2). Synthesis was overdue.

### What's new

`wiki/concepts/mcp-tool-marketplace.md` — meta-rubric similar in shape to `concepts/high-signal-paper-patterns.md` but for MCP servers + Claude Code skills rather than research papers.

### Five cross-cutting patterns synthesized

1. **License distribution skews MIT** (9 of 13 MCP servers MIT; 1 Apache-2.0; 1 NOASSERTION/Family C; 0 Family A — copyleft hasn't appeared yet but rubric should expect it)
2. **Maturity bimodal** — big-org high-star Adopts (qlib 40k★ / context7 53k★ / playwright-mcp 31k★) OR solo-author small-star Adopts (kukapay servers 3-40★ / 7onez 78★ / claudectl 11★ but 44 releases). **No middle ground in our inventory.**
3. **Vendor-dependency is a separate axis from license** — context7 has clean MIT client + proprietary Upstash backend (service-availability single point of failure). License axis ≠ deployment-risk axis.
4. **Cemini-stack vs Workflow scope is the operational dichotomy** — same MCP server can be Adopt for one and Defer/Reject for the other (vibe-trading, tradingview-mcp). Headless / 24-7 / TOS-rigorous (Cemini-stack) vs interactive / on-demand / experimentation-friendly (Workflow).
5. **Family C TOS-conflict is a recurring MCP-specific risk** — MCP servers bridging to vendor services inherit those services' TOS as deployment risk regardless of MCP code license. Worked example: tradingview-mcp.

### Adoption rubric (operational artifact)

- **Filter 1 (License + TOS posture)** — code license MIT/Apache/BSD AND third-party-data-TOS clear OR no third-party data
- **Filter 2 (Surface fit)** — Cemini-stack vs Workflow vs Librarian based on headless/latency/TOS-rigor profile
- **Filter 3 (Bus factor)** — big-org → follow upstream; mid-tier → pin commit, watch quarterly; solo-author → fork-and-maintain in-house; single-day-dev → skip OR reimplement
- **Quality signals** (4 hits ≥ 3 = strong Adopt) — verifiable license, reproducibility hooks, Cemini-domain-aligned tool surface, FastMCP-compatible

### Bidirectional updates (19 pages)

The new concept page references 17 source pages + 4 concept pages + 1 entity page. All 19 received the new concept in their related: lists. Lint run confirms 0 bidirectional gaps post-edit.

### Index + log

- `wiki/index.md` — added row in Concepts → Librarian / knowledge-base tooling section (after high-signal-paper-patterns, before Trading strategy concepts subsection)
- This log entry

### Operational impact (Cemini-pragmatic)

The rubric is **applied during every MCP-server evaluation** going forward. Specifically:
- Workflow A Tier-2 sweep results that surface MCP servers (which they typically do) get scored against this rubric in addition to the high-signal-paper-patterns rubric
- Future Gemini DR brief waves' MCP-server URLs get filtered through this rubric before recommending Adopt/Defer/Reject
- The integration architecture section informs `agents/orchestrator.py` MCP-client wiring decisions on the cemini-prod side

### Open follow-ups

- **`wiki/entities/components/cemini-mcp-registry.md`** roadmap candidate — living document listing all currently-registered MCP servers in cemini-prod's mcp_client config + per-server schema/status/version-pin/escalation contact. Maintained alongside production deployments. ~1 hour to draft skeleton; lives on cemini-prod-side execution after that.
- **Per-MCP-server health check extension** — extend `scripts/librarian_healthcheck.sh` to probe each registered MCP server on the same 5-min cron. ~30 min when ready.
- **Quarterly MCP-server review ritual** — every 90 days walk the registry: license re-check / upstream-still-maintained check / deprecation candidates. CronCreate candidate.
- **Backfill MCP-marketplace cross-link** to older MCP-related sources (kepano-obsidian-skills, bare-bones-memory, falkordb-graphrag-eval) — those don't currently reference the new concept; not strict gaps but worth a sweep.

### New Family in `concepts/ip-sale-readiness.md`: Family C — TOS-conflict via clean-license code

Added Family C to the license poison-pill ledger:
- **Family A** (existing) — Copyleft (GPL-3.0/AGPL-3.0/SSPL/ELv2)
- **Family B** (existing, 2026-04-29 from openclaude) — Vendor-IP-redistribution-without-authorization
- **Family C** (new, 2026-04-29 from tradingview-mcp) — Third-party-data-provider-TOS-conflict via clean-license code

Family C: code license is genuinely clean (typically MIT) but the documented use case requires interacting with a third-party provider's service in a way that may violate the provider's TOS. Worked example: tradingview-mcp uses Chrome DevTools Protocol against TradingView Desktop; README warns about TOS conflict for automated-trading use.

Detection heuristic extended: README contains warning text ("may violate", "users are responsible") + Chrome DevTools Protocol / undocumented APIs / web-scraping → automatic Family C flag. Mitigation: extract the code-pattern as Steal-from; route data through TOS-cleared providers (Polygon, Coinbase, Alpaca).

## [2026-04-29] ingest | TF-GRPO (closes AHE citation triangle) + LLMs Corrupt Your Documents (production-safety paper)

User dropped 6 inbox items late session: 4 papers + 2 Gemini DR briefs (Wave 4). Triage applied the `@concepts/high-signal-paper-patterns.md` rubric:

- **Ingest now**: TF-GRPO (closes AHE citation triangle) + LLMs Corrupt Your Documents (production-safety for delegated work)
- **Skip per Filter 3**: Epicure (food/flavor embeddings — domain mismatch)
- **Steal-from**: Usable Agent Discovery for Decentralized AI Systems (decentralized topology distant from Cemini's centralized stack)
- **Defer to dedicated session**: Wave 4 Gemini DR brief pair (substantial multi-URL evaluation, 2-4 hour ingest)

### Papers ingested

**1. Training-Free Group Relative Policy Optimization (TF-GRPO)** — arXiv 2510.08191, Cai et al (Tencent Youtu-Agent Team), Oct 9, 2025. New file: `wiki/sources/training-free-grpo-cai-2025.md`. **Closes the AHE citation triangle** — TF-GRPO is the 72.3% Terminal-Bench-2 baseline AHE compared against. Refutes "agentic RL needs parameter updates" — leaves LLM weights frozen, evolves an external experience library `E` as a token prior. **Semantic group advantage** replaces numerical advantage; LLM introspects rollouts and produces natural-language experiences via Add/Delete/Modify/Keep operations. Validated on DeepSeek-V3.1-Terminus (Cemini's predecessor brain). CC BY 4.0; code at github.com/TencentCloudADP/youtu-agent/tree/training_free_GRPO.

**Cemini-direct**: this is the methodology that fits Cemini's API-only deployment constraint. Concrete deployment surface: replace static analyst-prompts in `agents/orchestrator.py` with experience-library-driven prompts; ~50-line addition.

**2. LLMs Corrupt Your Documents When You Delegate** — arXiv 2604.15597, Laban + Schnabel + Neville (Microsoft Research), Apr 17, 2026. New file: `wiki/sources/llms-corrupt-documents-laban-2026.md`. **DELEGATE-52** benchmark: 310 work environments × 52 professional domains × 19 LLMs. **Refutes** "delegated work works as a paradigm" — even frontier models (Gemini 3.1 Pro / Claude 4.6 Opus / GPT-5.4) corrupt **25% of document content** over 20 interactions; all-model average is 50%. Python is the only domain (of 52) where most models reach the 98% "ready" threshold. **Round-trip relay methodology** + domain-specific parsers (LLM-as-judge captures only 25% of variance — proven inadequate). DeepSeek NOT in the 19-model panel.

**Cemini-direct caution**: Cemini's analyst workload (earnings calls, regulatory documents, specialized financial reasoning) lives in the worst-performing "natural language and niche" category. **This is the production-safety paper for Cemini's current stack** — higher operational priority than AHE/IKP/TF-GRPO because it directly measures the failure mode of "delegate to DeepSeek; trust it; don't review". Implementation surface: round-trip-relay verification on critical paths; never let DeepSeek do >5 consecutive editing steps without a verification gate.

## [2026-04-29] decision + concept | Workflow C — high-signal paper patterns rubric

User observed that the last 4 ingests (BSIV, PTA, AHE, IKP) "have mostly been very influential" and asked how to seek out specifically papers like these. The answer was a 3-step plan agreed earlier in this session: (C) write the pattern doc, (A) run a one-shot Exa sweep using the pattern, (Tier 2 promotion) automate weekly if A surfaces useful candidates. This entry covers Workflow C.

### What's new

`wiki/concepts/high-signal-paper-patterns.md` — a decision rubric synthesizing what made the 4 recent landings high-signal:

- **Three hard filters** (any failure → skip): Format (research-paper-grade) / Recency (≤30d default for proactive search) / Cemini-domain overlap (one of: quant finance, LLM agent infrastructure, LLM brain capacity, market infrastructure, data infrastructure, audit/governance/IP)
- **6 quality signals**: empirical results, comprehensive prior-work integration, code release, reproducibility hooks, refutation-of-prior, methodology-not-training-recipe
- **3 operational signals**: concrete Cemini deployment surface, quantifiable expected gain, clear next-step
- **7 negative signals**: license poison-pill family A (GPL-3.0/AGPL-3.0/SSPL/ELv2), poison-pill family B (vendor-IP-redistribution-without-authorization), single-author-with-extraordinary-claims, ≥50% wiki overlap, domain mismatch, closed-source-preview-only, vendor whitepaper

| PTA | 4/6 | 3/3 | Read |

## [2026-04-29] ingest | Incompressible Knowledge Probes (arXiv 2604.24827, Bojie Li, Pine AI)

User dropped `Incompressible Knowledge Probes_ Estimating Black-Box LLM Parameter Counts via Factual Capacity.pdf` (1.6 MB, 79 pages). Detected by the freshly-built `scripts/preingest_check.py` (Tier 0) — verdict ✅ NEW (no arXiv-ID / DOI / URL / filename / title matches). Shipped same session as the Tier 0 + Tier 1 build.

### What's new

`wiki/sources/incompressible-knowledge-probes-li-2026.md` (1 new source page).

### Core thesis

- Closed-source frontier labs don't publish parameter counts; existing inference-economics estimates carry 2× uncertainty from confounds external to the model
- Information-theoretic bound: storing F facts requires ≥ F/(bits per parameter) weights → factual-recall ability lower-bounds parameter count
- IKP benchmark: 1,400 probes across 7 tiers (T1–T7), tier-assigned **empirically** by which of 6 landmark models answers correctly
- Calibrated on 89 open-weight models from 19 vendors (135M–1,600B params): R² = 0.917; LOO CV 68.5%/87.6% within 2×/3×
- For MoE: total params predict knowledge (R² = 0.79) >> active params (R² = 0.51) — important deployment finding
- **Densing-Law refutation**: time coefficient on IKP across 96 dated open-weight models is −0.0010/month (95% CI [−0.0031, +0.0008]) — indistinguishable from zero, rejecting Densing's +0.0117/month prediction at p < 10⁻¹⁵. **Factual capacity does not compress.** Benchmark saturation reflects the Densing-compressible part (procedural skill), not the Shannon-bounded part (factual recall).
- Knowledge fingerprinting: rare-fact Jaccard + **hallucination-similarity** (rate of agreement on **wrong** answers) distinguishes weight-sharing siblings, post-training lineages, and full retrains across closed-vendor families — without requiring weights.

### Frontier-model parameter estimates from the paper (Figure 1)

| Model | Estimated total params |
|---|---|
| Claude Haiku 4.5 | ~65B |
| Gemini 2.5 Flash | ~207B |
| GPT-5 Mini | ~410B |
| Gemini 2.5 Pro | ~1.2T |
| Claude Sonnet 4.6 | ~1.7T |
| Claude Opus 4.6 | ~5.3T |
| GPT-5.5 | ~9.7T |

Heavily safety-tuned models are conservative lower bounds (refusal hides "refused but known" capacity).

### Why directly Cemini-relevant

The calibration set **explicitly includes `deepseek-v3`, `deepseek-v4-flash`, `deepseek-v4-flash-think`, `deepseek-v4-pro-think`**. This means IKP gives Cemini three operational primitives:

1. **Tier-calibrated retrieval routing**: T1–T2 facts (common) DeepSeek answers; T6–T7 (10–50-citation researchers, obscure entities) require explicit retrieval. The "shrug → fall back to RAG" decision in `agents/orchestrator.py` can be tier-empirically calibrated.
2. **Procedural-vs-factual cost optimization**: switch to cheaper smaller models for parsing/SQL/dispatch (Densing applies); keep big models for ticker/entity recall (Densing does NOT apply).
3. **Silent-swap detector**: 50-probe T5–T7 daily cron + hallucination-similarity drift alert. Detects vendor quantization / rerouting / model-family changes for ~$0.04/day.

### Bidirectional updates (3 pages)

- `concepts/llm-agent-systems.md` — added to related: list (cluster hub)
- `entities/infrastructure/deepseek-api.md` — added related + Relations bullet specifically about the deepseek-v4-* calibration data + the three operational primitives. This is the most concrete cross-link in the page now.
- `sources/agentic-harness-engineering.md` — added related + Relations bullet. The two papers form a natural pair: AHE measures harness-level evolvability (the substrate around the brain); IKP measures factual capacity (the brain itself). Useful for "what should Cemini optimize: harness or brain?" decisions.

- `wiki/log.md` — this entry.

This was the **first ingest using the new Step 2 (preingest_check)**. Smooth — the script ran in <1s, output was clear and actionable, no false-positive duplicate flags. The overall ingest workflow felt tighter with the Step 0 dup check before "Read the source": confidence of NEW status before investing reading time. ## [2026-04-29] decision + tooling | Proactive search architecture (Tier 0 + Tier 1 shipped; Tier 2 deferred; Tier 3 rejected)

User raised three asks late in the session: (a) "wire the script to run on everything in research to be indexed before indexing"; (b) duplicate detection that survives months/years of corpus drift; (c) "auto ingesting portion … seek out information you feel is relevant then score it against Exa". This entry captures the design decision + what shipped tonight.

### Three-tier framing

| Tier | What | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 0 — pre-ingest dup check | runs over `research to be indexed/` before any source page is created | **shipped: `scripts/preingest_check.py`** |
| 1 — gap-detection lint | descriptive surface of 3 gap classes; produces candidate list | **shipped: `scripts/wiki_gap_detect.py`** |
| 2 — proactive Exa with user-gated commit | weekly per-cluster Exa search; user approves; LLM ingests | **deferred — try Tier 1 first** |
| 3 — fully autonomous outbound ingest | no per-item authorization | **rejected (drift / cost / trust)** |

### Tier 0 — `scripts/preingest_check.py` (~270 lines Python)

Walks `wiki/sources/*.md` and indexes:
- arXiv IDs (regex over body text)
- DOIs (regex)
- URLs (normalized: lowercase scheme/host, strip trailing slash, strip `.git`)
- Filenames in `Location:` Raw Concept fields
- Titles (frontmatter `title:` field, both exact-normalized and tokenized for Jaccard match)

For each file in `research to be indexed/`:
- Computes SHA-256
- Extracts title (PDF metadata via pypdf; docx via python-docx core_properties; md via frontmatter or first heading)
- Extracts arXiv IDs / DOIs / URLs from first 15 KB of content
- Matches against the index, reports verdict: NEW / LIKELY / DUPLICATE

Smoke-tested by re-fetching the AHE PDF (already ingested as `sources/agentic-harness-engineering.md`) into the inbox: correctly flagged ⛔ DUPLICATE via arXiv ID 2604.25850 + URL `github.com/china-qijizhifeng/agentic-harness-engineering`.

Known v1 limitation: arXiv-ID matching catches the ID anywhere on a wiki page, including cross-link bullets. So an ingested paper with arXiv ID X may appear as a "match" for any other page that mentions X in a Relations or Narrative bullet. The canonical source page is always the strongest/first match; secondary matches are obvious to a human reader as cross-link mentions.

Wired into CLAUDE.md as the new ingest workflow Step 2.

### Tier 1 — `scripts/wiki_gap_detect.py` (~210 lines Python)

Surfaces three gap classes with proposed Exa-tool actions per item:

**Type A — Cited-unread stubs ready for deep-read** (`read_status=unread-stub` + ≥2 inbound). For each, proposes either `crawling_exa(url=…)` (if a URL is in the Raw Concept) or `web_search_exa(query=<title>, numResults=3)`. First run: **43 candidates**, top by inbound count: `gemini-dr-link-eval-2026-04-28.md` (17 inbound), `omc-talent-container-paper.md` (11 inbound), `vibe-trading-hkuds.md` (10 inbound).

**Type D — Stale `[NEEDS VERIFICATION YYYY-MM-DD]` tags ≥7 days**. Extracts the surrounding claim sentence; proposes `web_search_exa` then `crawling_exa` for primary-source verification. First run: **0 candidates** (correct — the 18 tags I dated this morning all hit the 7-day mark on 2026-05-06).

**Type E — Thin concept pages** (`## Narrative` <100 words). Lists inbound source pages as deep-read targets to flesh out the narrative. First run: **4 candidates**: `concepts/goldstein-scale.md` (65w), `concepts/cameo-codes.md` (68w), `concepts/carry-trade.md` (74w), `concepts/llm-agent-systems.md` (91w with **72 inbound citations** — biggest mismatch in the wiki).

Tier 1 is descriptive only. The user picks targets; the LLM (next session) runs the proposed Exa query / deep-read.

### CLAUDE.md updates

- Ingest workflow Step 2 added (preingest_check)
- Step numbers 2-10 renumbered to 3-11
- New `### Gap detection` subsection added under Operations, documents the script + tier framing

### ROADMAP.md updates

- New "Proactive search & gap-detection (3-tier design)" subsection under Eventually, with table tracking all 4 tiers (0, 1, 2, 3) and their statuses + transition criteria

### Open follow-ups

1. **Use Tier 1 for a week or two** before deciding on Tier 2. If gap-finding alone closes the "what should I ingest next?" question, Tier 2 may be unnecessary. If gaps surface but go un-filled, green-light Tier 2.
2. **Iterate preingest_check arXiv-ID precision**: restrict the regex to Raw Concept block only, OR rank matches by where-in-page they appear. Cosmetic; current output is human-readable and correctly identifies the canonical match first. Defer until annoyance materializes.
3. **Add `sha256:` field to source page frontmatter at ingest time** so dup-check can match by file hash directly (catches "renamed file, same content"). Backfill not necessary; let it accumulate forward.

## [2026-04-29] lint | full pass + tooling-tightening + tag dating

User prompt: "run lint on everything then commit and push" before next ingest. Pass surfaced two findings:

1. **23 undated `[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` tags** — actionable; lint can't track staleness without a date.
2. **144 cited-unread source stubs** — long-tail backlog (unchanged from prior pass).

### Categorization of the 23

After grep + per-line read:
- **18 real tags** = open verification claims (DeepSeek+langextract compat, playwright-mcp truncation, sentrux headless rendering, etc.) — dated `2026-04-29`.
- **5 false positives** = the literal string `` `[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` `` *describing the tag pattern itself* inside backticks (e.g. lint docstring quotes in `concepts/wiki-tooling-evaluation.md`, the falkordb-graphrag-eval example query, the atomicmemory-llm-wiki-compiler analog mention). These should never have been flagged.

### Tooling fix

`scripts/wiki_lint.py` line 163:
- old: `r"\[NEEDS VERIFICATION\](?!\s+\d{4})"`
- new: `r"(?<!\`)\[NEEDS VERIFICATION\](?!\`)(?!\s+\d{4})"`

## [2026-04-29] decision | LSA Phase 6 (monitoring + tuning) — closed out

After K9 ship, user prompted: "did we do phase 6?" — answer was no, Phase 6 was the only LSA phase still marked "ongoing" rather than DONE. Closed it out:

### What shipped (workspace-side)

**Phase 6a — librarian instrumentation**:
- `node_exporter` v1.8.2 installed on cemini-librarian; systemd-managed (`node_exporter.service`); bound to `10.0.0.2:9100` (private-network only, no public exposure even before firewall rule updates)
- Laptop-side `scripts/librarian_healthcheck.sh` (84 lines bash) doing 5-min cron probe of both kb-server `/api/v1/health` and node_exporter `/metrics`; pulls load1/mem_avail/disk_avail from node_exporter and logs to `~/Library/Logs/cemini-librarian-health.log` with ERROR exit codes for each failure mode (1=kb-server / 2=node_exporter / 3=both); cron entry `*/5 * * * *` installed on laptop crontab; first run green (load1=0.01 mem_avail=3.2GB disk_avail=61.1GB)

**Phase 6b — weekly Hetzner snapshots (infrastructure staged, gated on user)**:
- `/opt/scripts/librarian_weekly_snapshot.sh` (lives on librarian; calls Hetzner Cloud API directly via curl; no hcloud CLI dependency)
- `/etc/systemd/system/librarian-weekly-snapshot.{service,timer}` — runs Sunday 03:30 UTC + RandomizedDelaySec=600
- Retention: rolling 4 weeks (script auto-deletes >4-week-old snapshots matching `weekly-cemini-librarian-*` prefix). Cost ~€0.95/mo at 80 GB × 4 snapshots
- **Gated on user adding Hetzner Cloud API token** at `/etc/hetzner/api-token` and enabling the systemd timer. Documented in script header + Phase 6c brief stage-2 section

**Phase 6c — brief to cemini-prod**:
- `briefs/2026-04-29_librarian-observability-prod-integration.md` (295 lines) shipped to cemini-prod:/opt/cemini/briefs/
- Sections: pre-existing librarian surface table, Hetzner Cloud Firewall prerequisite (add port 9100 inbound from 10.0.0.0/16), 2 Prometheus scrape jobs (`cemini-librarian-node` + `cemini-librarian-kb-server-blackbox`), Promtail install on librarian (sketched bash) forwarding kb-server.log + librarian-weekly-snapshot.log + systemd-journal to Loki on cemini-prod 10.0.0.3:3100, 3 Grafana dashboards (librarian system health / kb-server activity / cross-server LSA migration health), 4 Prometheus alerting rules (LibrarianDown / LibrarianDiskNearFull / LibrarianMemoryPressure / LibrarianHighLoad), verification plan, stage-2 user-enable instructions for the snapshot timer

### What's left for Phase 6 closure

1. cemini-prod-side Claude Code session reads + executes the Phase 6c brief
2. User adds Hetzner Cloud API token + enables snapshot timer (stage-2 of Phase 6b)
3. After both: workspace mirrors `[ADOPTED]` on the LSA concept page Phase 6 row

### LSA migration arc — full closeout summary

| Phase | Status | Effort actuals |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Smoke-test llm-wiki-compiler / determine serving layer | ✅ DONE 2026-04-29 (~3 hr) |
| 1 | Provision Hetzner CPX21 in Ashburn | ✅ DONE 2026-04-28 (~30 min) |
| 2 | Initial wiki sync | ✅ DONE 2026-04-28 (~5 min) |
| 3 | Server-side serving stack (kb-server) | ✅ DONE 2026-04-29 (~3 hr) |
| 3.5 | kb-server hardening | ✅ DONE 2026-04-29 (~3 hr) |
| 4 | Wire production brain → librarian MCP | brief shipped to cemini-prod 2026-04-29 (server-stack scope from there) |
| 5 | Sync cron | ✅ DONE 2026-04-29 (folded into Phase 3.5; ~30 min) |
| 6 | Monitoring + tuning | ✅ DONE 2026-04-29 workspace-side; brief shipped to cemini-prod (~3 hr) |

**Total workspace-side effort** to take LSA from `[DECISION]` → fully built + monitored: ~13 hours over a single session 2026-04-28 → 2026-04-29. The original LSA migration plan estimated several days for Phases 4-6 (server-stack scope in particular); the workspace-side portion is now complete.

### Pattern note

Phase 6 was the easiest LSA phase to skip-and-pretend-done because it had no clear "done" marker — the original phase definition said "gated on Phase 1; ongoing." User's "did we do phase 6?" surfaced this as a real concrete deliverable rather than a vague "ongoing" task. **Lesson**: phases marked "ongoing" need explicit completion criteria or they become indefinite. For the LSA migration, the explicit Phase 6 done-criteria are now: librarian instrumented + laptop-side health-check active + weekly snapshot infrastructure staged + Phase 6c brief shipped to cemini-prod. Anything more (actual Grafana dashboards built, alerts tested, snapshot timer enabled with user's API token) is server-stack-side work the workspace doesn't directly own.

- **Cross-doc duplicate**: same source surfaces in 2+ corpus docs → existing concept-page promotion is correct; reinforcement note suffices, no new artefact
- **Cross-wave reinforcement**: same source surfaces across waves with new usage detail → cross-link the new detail back to the original wave's source page; no new artefact

These augment the K-series taxonomy (single-source-promote vs single-source-snippets-only) with cross-source-pattern-detection.

## [2026-04-28] decision | LSA — Librarian Server Architecture (dedicated Hetzner CPX21 Ashburn $13.99/mo, 24/7 SLA, six-phase migration)

User-committed 2026-04-28 directly downstream of the WTE (Wiki & Memory Tooling Evaluation) wave shipped earlier the same day. The trigger was a single sentence that reframed the librarian: *"it is going to be of utmost importance that the deployed cemini brain can query the library at any time as crypto markets are 24/7 and who knows when its going to need info."* This shifts the workspace librarian from "research/curation tool" (laptop-only model that worked through K1-K8 + WTE) to **operational dependency** for the deployed trading stack — and that reframe forces a hosting decision that laptop-as-host cannot satisfy.

### What shipped

**1 new concept page**: `wiki/concepts/librarian-server-architecture.md` (`maturity: validated`):
- **Architectural decision**: `[DECISION 2026-04-28]` Option C from the WTE "Hosting topology" sub-decision — dedicated Hetzner CPX21 librarian server (Ashburn, $13.99/mo)
- **4 alternatives explicitly rejected** with reasons:
  - Option A (laptop-as-MCP-host): laptop sleeps / drops wifi / IP rotates → fails 24/7 SLA
  - Option B (sync to existing production server): adds ~700 MB RAM resident to already-tight 7 GB box per K8 finding; couples librarian-failure to trading-stack-failure on shared infra
  - Option D (self-host Pi5/Mac mini/NUC at home): home ISP reliability dependency; the "laptop but bigger" problem
  - Migrate wiki to existing production server: same RAM constraint as Option B + breaks librarian doctrine ("the wiki lives on this laptop precisely because the server cannot carry the full knowledge-and-LLM loop locally" per `retracted_deepseek_ollama.md` memory file)
- **Three-server topology diagram**: laptop (canonical editor + Claude Code + Obsidian + git canonical) → librarian server (synced wiki copy + llm-wiki-compiler + Ollama + nomic-embed-text + MCP serve) → production trading-stack server (brain consumes librarian MCP). All three on Hetzner Cloud (intra-DC low-latency, unified ops, server-class reliability)
- **CPX-tier sizing math**: CPX21 (3 vCPU / 4 GB RAM / 80 GB NVMe / 2 TB traffic / $13.99): ~600 MB-1 GB OS + ~500 MB Ollama+nomic-embed-text + ~300-500 MB Node+llm-wiki-compiler = ~1.4-2 GB resident → ~2-2.6 GB headroom. **Sufficient for stated embeddings-only spec.** CPX31 ($24.99) as snapshot-based upgrade path if usage signals chat-model-on-librarian needed
- **Cross-provider survey**: OVHcloud / Vultr / Linode / DigitalOcean / Contabo / Oracle Free Tier — all evaluated; Hetzner-same-vendor-as-production selected for unified ops + intra-DC latency + competitive pricing
- **6-phase migration plan**: Phase 0 (WTE-1 smoke test on laptop, gates everything) → Phase 1 (provision CPX21 in Ashburn) → Phase 2 (initial wiki sync, rsync-via-SSH) → Phase 3 (server-side `llmwiki compile` + `llmwiki serve` MCP as systemd service) → Phase 4 (wire production brain → librarian MCP — server-stack scope, stages in briefs/) → Phase 5 (sync cron + file-watch incremental compile) → Phase 6 (monitoring + tuning + weekly Hetzner snapshots)
- **Sync model table**: what syncs (`wiki/`) vs what doesn't (`research for cemini/` raw sources stay laptop-only; `briefs/` uses existing scp-to-prod channel; `.claude/` stays local)
- **Operational SLA targets**: 99.9%+ availability (Hetzner Cloud SLA matches), <1 hr recovery via snapshot rebuild, p99 MCP query latency <500 ms intra-DC, ≤1 hr sync staleness on hourly cron, ≥4 weeks snapshot retention

### Pattern note

LSA is the **first 24/7-SLA-driven decision** captured on the wiki, and the **first decision that adds infrastructure rather than just choosing existing tools**. Sets a precedent for capturing operational-dependency decisions as concept-page-as-decision-document — parallel to WTE's tool-adoption-decision-document pattern shipped in the same commit window. Crypto-market-driven 24/7 framing as the load-bearing constraint is also new in this corpus — previous reliability framing was "curation convenience," not "deployed-trading-stack uptime." Future similar decisions (monitoring stack expansion, redundant pgvector, multi-region failover, observability for the librarian server itself) likely follow the same shape: concept-page-as-decision-document with explicit alternatives + rejection reasons + sizing math + migration phases + operational SLA targets.

The **decision sequence within a single working session** is also worth noting: WTE shipped (3f923e6) → user posed pricing/Ollama questions → DECISION clarification commit shipped (192319e) → user surfaced 24/7 reliability requirement → architectural reframe to dedicated server → LSA shipped (this commit). This is fast-iteration decision-making, with each step properly captured before the next surfaces. The wiki acts as durable thinking-rest-state between turns; without it, the same decision-tree would have to be re-derived every session.

## [2026-04-28] ingest | WTE — Wiki & Memory Tooling Evaluation (7 user-dropped candidates → 1 concept page + 6 source pages)

User dropped 7 URLs across two turns 2026-04-28 for evaluation against the workspace librarian's current Karpathy-pattern coverage and the prescribed-not-built cpr-pattern wiring path. Direct continuation of the K8 ROADMAP expansion's "Operational efficiency toward Karpathy LLM Wiki standard" subsection.

### What shipped

**1 new concept page** — `wiki/concepts/wiki-tooling-evaluation.md` (`maturity: validated`):
- Synthesis comparing 7 candidates (FalkorDB / agentmemory / atomicmemory llm-wiki-compiler+llmwiki.app / knowledge-base-server / memex / emtwiki / hand-rolled status quo) against the schema in `CLAUDE.md` and the K8-expanded ROADMAP open questions
- Coverage matrix: workspace status quo at **4-of-10 Karpathy operations**; llm-wiki-compiler at **7-of-10**; memex 5-of-10; knowledge-base-server 6-of-10; agentmemory 5-of-10
- Architectural-axis split: filesystem-first (status quo, llm-wiki-compiler, memex, emtwiki) vs database-first (kb-server, FalkorDB, agentmemory) — Cemini-side preference is filesystem-first (git is the version-control story; Markdown is the universal format; database-vs-markdown drift is operational complexity)
- Recommendation by tier: **Adopt** llm-wiki-compiler (MIT, MCP server, hash-incremental compile, candidate review queue, confidence + contradictedBy metadata) for workspace librarian / **Steal-from** agentmemory (Apache-2.0 pipeline shape) for deployed-stack cpr-pattern Python port / **Defer** FalkorDB (SSPL poison-pill on deployed stack — workspace-side-only experiment OK) / **Defer** knowledge-base-server (multi-agent gated) / **Reject** memex (Linux/CAP_SYS_ADMIN friction) / **Reference-only** emtwiki (deployed example proof)
- 5 explicit decision gates that would flip the recommendation (overwrite-of-hand-curated-frontmatter, prohibitive LLM cost, MCP retrieval poor, Python-stack preference, sixth/seventh candidate emerges)
- Forward-pointer to the K8 ROADMAP "Memory storage / reading / indexing" subsection as the implementation-target for the steal-from-agentmemory pipeline

**6 new source pages** — each `read_status: validated`, `clusters: [llm-agents]`, with substantive Narrative + verbatim Snippets:
- `wiki/sources/atomicmemory-llm-wiki-compiler.md` — covers github + llmwiki.app landing page; primary adoption candidate; 7-of-10 Karpathy ops; MIT; multi-provider; MCP server (`llmwiki serve` — 7 tools + 5 resources); confidence/contradictedBy/inferredParagraphs metadata; candidate review queue (`--review` mode)
- `wiki/sources/willynikes2-knowledge-base-server.md` — JS+SQLite-FTS5+Xenova-embeddings memory engine + 16-tool MCP; three-tier (hot/warm/cold) memory framing; Memstalker hosted-SaaS shadow; deferred-for-adoption (sidecar value gated on multi-agent need)

### ROADMAP updates

### Pattern note

WTE is the **first concept-page-as-decision-document** in the wiki — not a research-synthesis (like Q's corpus-index hubs), not a doctrine page (like ip-sale-readiness), and not a deep-read promotion (like the K-series). It's an **adoption-evaluation document** intended to *update* and ultimately *resolve* — the recommendation row gets stamped `[ADOPTED 2026-04-XX]` with real-world cost numbers if the smoke test passes, OR the page becomes the retraction-of-record explaining "why we kept hand-rolling" if it fails. Sets a precedent: when conductor decision lands (Fugu / Trinity / Conductor / status quo), the same concept-page-as-decision-document shape may be the right home rather than piling rows into ROADMAP. The user's framing on this turn — *"if you prefer a different document than roadmap for this kind of stuff make that as well"* — explicitly authorized this pattern, and the librarian doctrine in `CLAUDE.md` ("File answers back: if the query produced a valuable synthesis, file it as a new concept page or brief") backs it. Also notable: this is the **first ingest where 6 source pages land alongside 1 concept page in a single wave** — closer to a thematic-cluster ingest (like the F sweep's per-ticker briefs) than a single-source K-style read, but with much heavier synthesis. Worth tracking to see if this shape repeats.
