## [2026-04-28] deep-read | K8 — `sources/4-5-links.md` Cemini-internal multi-topic evaluation (13 GitHub repos + 1 academic-policy synthesis)

K-series continuation after K7 (`238c63d`) closed the corporate-structure trio. K8 target was selected by the next-most-cited routing heuristic: `sources/4-5-links.md` was the highest-citation cited-but-unread stub at 5 inbound. Document is a Cemini-internal evaluation of 13 GitHub repositories against the deployed Cemini stack on a fixed 8-section template (What is this? / Architecture patterns worth stealing / Algorithms or techniques worth evaluating / Code or tooling worth examining / What they got wrong or overcomplicated / IP sale relevance / Solo-dev filter / Conflicts with the deployed stack / Actionable Roadmap Directives), plus a 14th synthesis section pulling from `skills 1 4:5.pdf`, `skills 2 4:5.pdf`, `trading 3 4:5.pdf`, `trading 4:5.pdf`, `ai trading ethics 4:5.pdf`. Sibling document `4_5 links 2.docx` (12 additional H2 sections, same shape, same authoring date 2026-04-05, one duplicate cross-doc evaluation `EliaAlberti/cpr-compress-preserve-resume`) is **deferred to K9**.

### What shipped

- **`wiki/sources/4-5-links.md`** — full source-page rewrite. `read_status: validated`, expanded `related:` (15 entries), substantive 14-section Narrative covering each H2 evaluation + the academic-policy Synthesis. **Server-state calibration table** captured the strongest stale-snapshot finding to date in the K-series:
  - **128 GB RAM (doc claim)** vs **7 GB total RAM** (`free -g` 2026-04-28) — **~18× over actual**. Lineage = 2026-04-23 hetzner.md frontmatter NEEDS-VERIFICATION baseline, not first-hand server measurement.
  - **22 `@mcp.tool` decorators (doc claim)** vs **130 decorators** (server probe 2026-04-28) — **~6× under actual**.
  - **April 14-17 RL retrain window (doc claim, "MAPPO retrain happens this week")** vs **logic-audit-and-bugfix work in that window** (no MAPPO retrain). Doc captured planned-but-not-executed work.
  - **40 deployed containers** (consistent with K5/K6 server probes 2026-04-26 / 2026-04-27).
  - **LangGraph checkpointer NOT WIRED** — neither `PostgresSaver` nor `MemorySaver` is invoked anywhere in `agents/orchestrator.py`. Doc's recommendation to wire one for the cpr-pattern compression node is prescribed-not-built.

### Promotion discipline (3 of 13 H2 sections promoted to canonical pages)

- **`wiki/entities/frameworks/autoagent.md`** — NEW entity page. **Score-Driven Hill Climbing** orchestration pattern (deterministic score → exploit; ambiguous score → explore) + **Single-File Registry-Driven Harness** code architecture (the entire workflow registry sits in one `registry.py` file as a Python list — opposite of LangGraph's distributed-decorators-across-files pattern; collapses cognitive surface area for solo-dev review). MIT 🟢 license. NOT-deployed status documented; cluster-routes to `concepts/llm-agent-systems.md`. Promotion rationale: the two patterns are reusable architectural primitives that don't fit cleanly under any existing concept hub.
- **`wiki/concepts/context-compression-resume-pattern.md`** — NEW concept page. **Library-agnostic 3-phase pattern** (Compress: LLM-summarizes-rolling-window; Preserve: pgvector + tamper-anchored audit hash; Resume: bi-directional save/load). Distinct from forward-only-append cryptographic-audit-trail (cpr is read-write — debate trajectories get *replayed* into context windows, not just verified). Cross-doc duplicate evaluation in `4_5 links 2.docx`. Promotion rationale: a pattern, not a library — `EliaAlberti/cpr-compress-preserve-resume` is the reference implementation (PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 🔴 — toxic for IP sale; pattern itself is reusable in-house under a clean license).
- **`wiki/entities/infrastructure/localai.md`** — NEW entity page. **Standardized API Shim** architecture pattern (OpenAI-compatible REST endpoint as portable abstraction over llama.cpp + GGUF). MIT 🟢 license. CPU-optimized + GGUF-quantized mandatory under the 7 GB RAM constraint (not optional — 14B-FP16 weights alone ~28 GB before the inference overhead). NOT-deployed status; sibling to the retracted ollama.md / deepseek-r1-14b.md. Promotion rationale: when local-inference hardware lands, this is the architectural slot — swap `base_url="https://api.deepseek.com"` for `base_url="http://localai:8080"` with zero Python orchestration changes. Cross-link to the cpr-pattern compression node.

### Snippets-only (10 of 13 H2 sections retained as source-page Snippets, no canonical promotion)

- `lukechampine/blob` (CC0 🟢) — ergonomic Go blob primitive; not Python-stack-relevant.
- `gjbex/Python-for-Data-Science` (CC-BY-NC-SA 🔴) — pedagogical material; license-toxic.
- `LongFlowSimulation/lfs-engine` (LGPL-3.0 🟡) — hydrodynamics; off-topic for finance stack.
- `microsoft/LIDA` (MIT 🟢) — auto-visualization library; promising but solo-dev-filter rejected (Streamlit-replacement scope).
- `huggingface/setfit` (Apache-2.0 🟢) — few-shot text classification; FinBERT-replacement candidate but currently no FinBERT in deployed stack (the K3 Telegram retraction stripped it out).
- `BerriAI/litellm` (MIT 🟢) — LLM proxy; valuable as a future routing layer, but the deployed `services/llm_analyst/deepseek_client.py` is already a thin OpenAI-SDK wrapper, integration-cost = familiar idiom; defer.
- `vacovaco/llmstxt` (MIT 🟢) — site-instructions for LLM crawlers; no Cemini surface area exposed for crawlers yet.
- `hwchase17/langchain-chatbot` (MIT 🟢) — LangChain reference; LangGraph already operative, LangChain integration would be churn.
- `langchain-ai/langgraph-supervisor-py` (MIT 🟢) — supervisor recipe library; pulled patterns inline into `concepts/scatter-gather-topology.md` historically; doesn't need its own page.
- `eosphoros-ai/DB-GPT` (AGPL-3.0 🔴) — multi-agent data-app platform; AGPL is institutional-acquirer poison.

### Bidirectional backlinks (10 cluster + entity touches)

`entities/frameworks/langgraph.md` (autoagent + cpr-pattern + 4-5-links — checkpointer-not-wired calibration), `entities/frameworks/fastmcp.md` (autoagent + 4-5-links — 130-tool calibration), `concepts/scatter-gather-topology.md` (all 3 K8 promotions — autoagent registry pattern, cpr cross-debate state, localai swap-in), `concepts/llm-agent-systems.md` (autoagent + cpr-pattern), `concepts/cryptographic-audit-trail.md` (cpr-pattern — forward-only-append vs bidirectional-save/load distinction), `concepts/ip-sale-readiness.md` (autoagent — solo-dev-filter pattern + localai — portable LLM-runtime abstraction), `entities/infrastructure/hetzner.md` (localai — 7 GB RAM constraint amplifies the CPU-optimized story), `entities/infrastructure/deepseek-api.md` (cpr-pattern — Compress-phase summarization target + localai — same OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions schema), `entities/infrastructure/ollama.md` (localai — peer alternative for the same retracted local-runtime slot), `entities/infrastructure/deepseek-r1-14b.md` (localai — candidate runtime for hosting GGUF weights *if* hardware lands).

### Pattern note

K8 is the **strongest stale-snapshot finding** in the K-series. Where K3/K4 caught prescription-as-as-built collapses on Cemini-internal blueprint sources, and K5/K6/K7 caught smaller cross-source corroboration deltas, K8 caught a 23-day-old internal evaluation that captured **18× over** on RAM, **6× under** on FastMCP tools, and a **planned-but-not-executed RL retrain window**. Lineage analysis: the 128 GB RAM figure traces back through hetzner.md frontmatter (created 2026-04-23) — never first-hand server measurement, and was never re-verified before being inherited by the 4-5-links.md doc author. K8 also formally introduces the **Promotion vs Snippets-only discipline** as a K-series practice: 3-of-13 H2 sections promoted because their patterns (Score-Driven Hill Climbing, Single-File Registry, cpr 3-phase, Standardized API Shim) are reusable architectural primitives; 10-of-13 retained as source-page Snippets only because their value is captured by paraphrase + license-tag without warranting a canonical page. Future K-series passes on multi-topic link-aggregate docs (the `4-4-link-re*` / `4-3-link-re*` / `4-6-links` / `3-26-link-re-3` / `3-28-links-2` queue at 3-inbound each) can adopt this discipline directly.

## [2026-04-27] ingest | Sakana Fugu (closed beta, productization of Conductor + Trinity)

User dropped `https://sakana.ai/fugu-beta/` 2026-04-27 as follow-up to the Conductor paper ingest the same day, with the redirect "wrong link [from `conductor.build`] but worth checking that one too". Identified as the **commercial productization of the Conductor + Trinity ICLR 2026 papers**, launched 2026-04-24 (three days before this entry). The Fugu ingest is structurally the **first vendor+commercial-API entity ingest in the corpus** (vs prior infrastructure entities being either deployed Cemini stack or retracted-options pages). ## [2026-04-27] structural | R: read_status frontmatter migration — 238 source pages backfilled, lint signal restored

Closes a stale lint signal-vs-noise issue surfaced while picking the next K-series deep-read target. Section 5 of `wiki_lint.py` ("cited but unread") routes on the `read_status` *frontmatter* field; but 102 source pages had their `read_status` only in body bullets (`- **Read status**: deep-read`) — the frontmatter was empty for that field, so the lint counted them as unread despite being validated deep-reads. Top-of-list noise was masking the real K-series candidates.

### What shipped

**Migration (238 source pages):**
- 102 pages with non-stub body status (deep-read, read-skim, read, validated): body status promoted into frontmatter
- 136 pages with no body status or body=`unread-stub`: frontmatter `read_status: unread-stub` added explicitly (for honesty — every source now has authoritative status, not "empty == assumed unread")
- Insertion point: after `maturity:` line if present, else before `created:`, else appended

**Stub-generator hygiene (`scripts/bootstrap_inbox.py` + `scripts/generate_stubs.py`):**
- Both scripts now emit `read_status: unread-stub` in frontmatter (in addition to the existing body bullet)
- Future bootstrap runs produce stubs that are immediately authoritative for `wiki_lint.py` without body-string parsing

### Lint impact

- **Before R**: 238 cited-unread (false signal — included all deep-read pages with empty `read_status` frontmatter)
- **After R**: 136 cited-unread (real signal — only genuinely unread stubs with ≥1 inbound citation)
- Top of real cited-unread queue: `sources/4-5-links.md` (4 inbound), `sources/4-4-link-re.md` (3 inbound), `sources/fintech-startup-legal-ip-roadmap.md` (3 inbound), `sources/3-26-link-re-3.md` (3 inbound). Numbers compressed; per-source citation density is the new sort axis (≤4 inbound at top, vs. pre-R apparent ceiling of 9 inbound from the 3-17/forex-research false positives).

### Pattern note

This is a librarian-frontmatter-vs-body divergence — same shape as the YAML-title-colon bug fixed in Q-tail (where stub generators wrote frontmatter the lint couldn't parse). General lesson: stub-generator scripts SHOULD emit machine-authoritative metadata in frontmatter even when the same fact also appears in human-readable body sections. Frontmatter is the single source of truth for tooling; body bullets are for human readers. They should agree on creation and stay in sync on promotion.

Followups:
- All other body-vs-frontmatter divergences (e.g. `Length: 14p` body vs `pages:` field) — defer until another field becomes load-bearing for tooling.
- "Promote to deep-read" workflow should set frontmatter directly (already implicit in how K-series page rewrites have been done recently).

## [2026-04-27] structural | Q-tail: preventative fix for YAML-title-colon bug at the source

Closes the Q follow-up loop. Both `scripts/bootstrap_inbox.py` (line 213, removed) and `scripts/generate_stubs.py` (line 157, removed) constructed YAML title lines via `f"title: {json.dumps(title, ensure_ascii=False)[1:-1]}\n"` — strips the JSON wrapper quotes leaving a YAML "plain scalar". Plain scalars cannot contain `:` characters; titles with embedded colons (very common — "X: A Y", "X: From Y to Z") broke `yaml.safe_load` on the resulting frontmatter and silently dropped those sources from any tooling that scans tags via parsed YAML.

Fix: replace the `json.dumps[1:-1]` line with a proper YAML single-quote scalar:

```python
title_yaml = "'" + title.replace("'", "''") + "'"
# ...
f"title: {title_yaml}\n"
```

Single-quote YAML preserves colons, smart quotes, unicode, and most punctuation verbatim; only the single quote itself needs escaping (doubled per spec). Round-trip identity verified against the same five problematic titles that broke pre-Q (curly quotes, embedded apostrophes, multi-colon stacks).

Also removed unused `import json` from `bootstrap_inbox.py` (was only referenced in the just-removed dumps call). `generate_stubs.py` keeps the import — it still uses `json.loads` for the corpus-cache file.

Future bootstrap runs into `wiki/sources/` will now produce parseable frontmatter on the first pass; `wiki_lint.py` and `build_cluster_hubs.py` won't need a remedial bulk-fix sweep.

## [2026-04-27] ingest | Q: 4 corpus-index hubs (quant-finance, market-microstructure, portfolio-strategy, options-derivatives) — 120 sources cross-linked + 58 YAML-title parse bugs fixed

Closes the cluster-bootstrap arc (D + D′ pattern → Q): the four remaining tagged-but-hub-less clusters now have index-only discovery hubs with bidirectional backlinks across every member source. Lint clean post-Q: **0 orphans / 0 bidir gaps / 0 dangling / 0 missing @paths** (was 0 / 76 / 0 / 0 immediately pre-Q because the colon-in-title YAML bug suppressed cluster routing for 58 sources).

### What shipped

- `scripts/build_cluster_hubs.py` — Q-task tooling. Mirrors O's update-hub pattern. Scans `wiki/sources/*.md` for `tags:` membership in any of `{quant-finance, market-microstructure, portfolio-strategy, options-derivatives}`, writes 4 hub pages with `## Relations` bullets, then walks each member source to append the hub path to `related:` and a Relations bullet.
- `wiki/concepts/quant-finance.md` (65 sources) — volatility forecasting, VaR/CVaR/drawdown, backtesting methodology, factor models, alpha decay
- `wiki/concepts/market-microstructure.md` (55 sources) — order-book dynamics, market-maker inventory, latency-arbitrage, HFT, ETF intraday liquidity
- `wiki/concepts/portfolio-strategy.md` (36 sources) — Sharpe / Sortino / Calmar, risk parity, mean-variance + Black-Litterman, Kelly sizing, rebalance / turnover
- `wiki/concepts/options-derivatives.md` (23 sources) — American/European pricing, rough-vol & fractional models, delta/gamma/vega hedging, variance swaps, FRTB-audit applications
- 120 unique source pages updated with one-to-many backlinks (most sources tag into 1–2 hubs; 7 tag into 3; 1 into all 4 — overlap is explicit and intentional)
- `wiki/index.md` — "Research corpus index hubs" section now lists all 8 hubs (LLM-agents · RL/MARL · AI-ethics · quant-finance · microstructure · portfolio · options · corpus-overflow)

### YAML-title-colon bug — preventative win

Mid-task surfaced via `wiki_lint.py`: 58 source pages had unquoted YAML titles containing `:` (e.g. `title: Cryptographic Verification Mechanisms for Algorithmic Trading Due Diligence: A Technical Audit Report`). YAML parses the second `:` as a mapping-key delimiter → entire frontmatter block fails `yaml.safe_load`. Root cause: `scripts/bootstrap_inbox.py` strips the JSON wrapper quotes via `[1:-1]` without re-quoting for YAML safety. Symptom: `build_cluster_hubs.py`'s `collect_members()` silently skipped these 58 sources (they show no `tags:` — frontmatter unparseable). 

In-place bulk fix: regex-wrap `title:` value in single quotes (preserving smart-quote characters inside). 58 fixed → all parse. Cluster-hub re-run picked them up cleanly. Future-proofing fix in `bootstrap_inbox.py` is a follow-up candidate but not gated on Q.

### Lint state at ship

```
Wiki lint scan — 359 pages indexed
Outbound edges: 1490; Inbound edge coverage: 359 pages
Orphans: 0 · Bidir gaps: 0 · Dangling: 0 · Missing @paths: 0
```

Cluster-bootstrap arc complete. Per-source promotion (stub → real read pass) remains the dominant outstanding hygiene work (238 cited-but-unread stubs, top of which is `forex-research-3-18.md` at 8 inbound / `3-17-competition-research.md` at 9 inbound — natural deep-read targets).

## [2026-04-27] tooling | M: Obsidian community plugins + markdown-link-check CI — install pass after Graphify/tool-survey

Resolves the tool-survey follow-up to the Graphify research turn. The survey returned three "Try" candidates worth installing (over Graphify itself, which was rejected as too heavy for a lean librarian aesthetic — Graph view + Dataview already cover its niche). Two are user-side Obsidian community plugins; one is a CI link-checker.

**Installed (user-side, vendored under `wiki/.obsidian/plugins/`):**

- **Obsidian Linter v1.31.2** (`wiki/.obsidian/plugins/obsidian-linter/`) — formats YAML frontmatter / heading spacing / list styles / footnotes. Useful as a hand-on-the-wheel for the schema's frontmatter conventions; complements `scripts/wiki_lint.py` (server-side structural lint) with editor-side cosmetic lint. Plugin id `obsidian-linter` matches dir name (verified via `manifest.json`).
- **Omnisearch v1.28.2** (`wiki/.obsidian/plugins/omnisearch/`) — full-text fuzzy search with ranking. Faster than Obsidian's built-in search on a 333-page corpus + handles the `@path/page.md` reference style better. Plugin id `omnisearch` matches dir name.

Both downloaded directly from GitHub release assets (`platers/obsidian-linter` 1.31.2 and `scambier/obsidian-omnisearch` 1.28.2). `.obsidian/` is gitignored (matches both workspace-root and `wiki/`-scoped vault directories), so these files exist on the local workspace only — they'll **not** commit. **User action required**: open Obsidian → Settings → Community plugins → enable both ("Linter" and "Omnisearch") after restart.

**Vault-location correction**: initial install dropped the plugins at the workspace-root `.obsidian/plugins/`, but the user's actual Obsidian vault is `wiki/` itself (evidence: `wiki/.obsidian/community-plugins.json` already lists `["dataview"]` and the live workspace state `wiki/.obsidian/workspace.json` is 4956 bytes vs. the workspace-root `.obsidian/` having an empty `community-plugins.json`). Plugins moved post-install via `mv .obsidian/plugins/<name> wiki/.obsidian/plugins/`; empty workspace-root `.obsidian/plugins/` removed. The `wiki/`-vault choice is consistent with where dataview is already installed (the L dashboard at `wiki/dashboard.md` lives there).

**Installed (CI-side, committed):**

- **`.github/workflows/links.yml`** — new GH Actions workflow running `tcort/markdown-link-check` against `wiki/`, `briefs/`, and root-level `.md` on monthly cron (`0 12 1 * *`) + manual dispatch only. **Not** on push: link checks make external HTTP requests and would create flaky-fail noise that would gate every commit. Modeled on the existing `lint.yml` pattern (informational, non-gating, posts to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`). Excludes `.obsidian/` vendored plugin trees from the file scan.
- **`.markdown-link-check.json`** — config file: ignores `mailto:`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `file://`, and the `@`-prefixed wiki-internal references (which aren't real URLs). Custom `User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; markdown-link-check)` header for `github.com` (without it, GitHub returns 406 for the bare domain when an over-specific `Accept` header is sent). 30s timeout, 429 retry, accept 200/206/301/302/303/307/308.

**Version pin gotcha**: `markdown-link-check@3.13.6` (latest) has a regression where invocation with `-c <config>` exits 1 silently with no stdout. Reproducible across empty config / minimal config / full config; works fine without `-c`. Falls back to **3.12.2** (last known-good with config support). Workflow YAML pins explicitly: `npm install -g markdown-link-check@3.12.2`. Local test confirmed: 3.12.2 + full config + real wiki file (`marketaux.md`, 3 external links) → exit 0 + all links alive.

**Implementation note** — dead-link counting: the tool emits `[✖]` glyph twice per failure (once inline during scan, once in `ERROR:` summary block). The workflow's `dead_links` counter uses `→ Status:` instead, which appears once per dead link in the summary block only. Avoids double-counting in the step-summary panel.

**Files**: 2 new files committed (`.github/workflows/links.yml`, `.markdown-link-check.json`); 6 files vendored under `wiki/.obsidian/plugins/{obsidian-linter,omnisearch}/` (gitignored, user-side only); 2 modified (`wiki/log.md`, `ROADMAP.md`).

## [2026-04-25] query+brief | C: post-Kalshi-advisory regulatory landscape — Exa pressure-test end-to-end

Closed roadmap item C. Exercised Exa MCP on a substantive research question — *"regulatory developments for prediction-market DCMs since the Feb 25 2026 CFTC Kalshi advisory"* — and filed the synthesis as `briefs/2026-04-25_post-kalshi-advisory-regulatory-update.md` (target: claude.ai).

### Doctrine pressure-test result

The doctrine in `CLAUDE.md` § "External research — Exa MCP" worked end-to-end:

1. **Wiki-first** — read `@sources/3-19-compliance-research.md` (S7, S8, S19 already populated with the Feb 25 advisory primary-source crawl from yesterday) + `@entities/platforms/kalshi.md` (3-role framing) before going external.
2. **Date-bounded `web_search_advanced_exa`** — 4 parallel queries, all bounded `2026-02-25 → 2026-04-25`, each ≤8 results. Persisted JSON outputs (Exa returned >50KB on 4 of 4) routed to disk by the harness; extracted via `python3 json.load` rather than re-querying.
3. **Cross-source corroboration** — every claim in the brief is sourced to either (a) primary federal docs (CFTC press releases, FR docket, Selig testimony transcript on cftc.gov), (b) state AG official press releases, or (c) ≥2-source corroborated press coverage of court orders.
4. **OOD signal honored** — when Selig testimony quoted an FR publication date that disagreed with my pre-summary memory (March 16 actual vs. March 12 in my notes), the brief uses the verified docket date and notes the distinction (CFTC announced March 12 / Press Release 9194-26; FR-published March 16 as 91 FR 12516, FR Doc 2026-05105).
5. **Filed back** — synthesis is in `briefs/`, not lost in chat. Ingest-back-to-wiki plan is documented in §6 of the brief but not executed (those are 3-4 separate small ingests; deferred).

### Brief content (one-paragraph summary)

Two months after the Feb 25 advisory, the prediction-market DCM regime moved from "advisory + cascading API-participant obligations" to a live federal-vs-state preemption fight. The CFTC issued a coordinated ANPRM (RIN 3038-AF65, 91 FR 12516, March 16) + DMO Staff Letter 26-08, opening a 45-day comment window closing **April 30 2026** (5 days from the brief). State enforcement clustered on sports + election contracts (MI civil March 5, AZ 20 misdemeanor criminal counts March 17, NV state-court TRO March 21, NY cease-and-desist reissued April 20). The federal government counter-sued AZ + CT + IL early April; Judge Liburdi (D. Ariz.) granted a TRO blocking AZ's criminal prosecution April 10, ruling CFTC "likely to ultimately win" on preemption (TRO expired April 24 — yesterday). Third Circuit upheld Kalshi's NJ injunction 2-1; a Sixth Circuit / Tennessee split signals a SCOTUS path. Selig testified before House Ag April 16 — held the line on exclusive jurisdiction but took bipartisan grilling on Trump-family insider-trading proximity. Implication for the Cemini compliance-leverage thesis (`@sources/3-19-compliance-research.md`): Kalshi's DCM-status as IP-sale leverage point **strengthens** if preemption holds (CFTC has demonstrated willingness to sue states to defend it) and **weakens** binarily if SCOTUS sides with states.

### Cost discipline

Exa spend: 4 advanced searches × `numResults` 5-8 each. No `deep_researcher_*`, no `crawling_exa` calls — yesterday's 2-URL crawl in S19 (CFTC Feb 25 advisory + govinfo FR ANPRM) already covered the primary-document layer. Estimated: well under $0.10 of API spend.

### Wiki-side follow-ups (queued, not blocking)

The brief flags 4 small ingests in §6 that would propagate the new findings back into the wiki (promote S7's ANPRM reference to S20 with full FR citation; new `concepts/dcm-state-preemption.md`; Selig testimony + Liburdi TRO as new source pages; update `kalshi.md` role 3 with the federal-counter-suit qualifier). Deferred — not executed in this session. They're a coherent ~4-6 page mini-cluster and should be one ingest, not folded into this commit.

Closed the dev-infra gap on the GitHub side. The wiki now has a CI lint pass and the local repo blocks the most likely accidental leaks before they leave the laptop.

### Three new artifacts

1. **`.github/workflows/lint.yml`** — runs `scripts/wiki_lint.py` on:
   - every push to `main`
   - weekly cron (Monday 12:00 UTC)
   - manual `workflow_dispatch`

   Output is `tee`'d to a file and posted to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` so the lint report renders as a markdown panel on the workflow run page (no need to dig into raw logs). Informational only — the lint script always exits 0, so the workflow is non-gating by design.

2. **`scripts/pre-commit.sh`** — bash hook that scans staged content and filenames before each commit. Blocks:
   - **Filenames**: `.env` / `.env.*` / `.DS_Store` at any depth
   - **Content (added lines only)**: literal Hetzner server IP (constructed from string parts so the hook source itself doesn't trip), `sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}` API key prefix (catches OpenAI / DeepSeek / Anthropic-style keys ≥20 chars after the `sk-`)

   Only ADDED lines (`git diff --cached -U0 | grep '^+'`) are scanned, so historical IP/key occurrences in untouched parts of files don't false-positive. The hook itself is excluded from content scanning via `:(exclude)scripts/pre-commit.sh`. Bypass when intentional: `git commit --no-verify`.

3. **`scripts/install-hooks.sh`** — idempotent installer that symlinks `.git/hooks/pre-commit → ../../scripts/pre-commit.sh`. Run once after fresh clone. Hooks live outside the repo's tracked tree, so this is the only way to propagate the hook to other machines / clones.

### Plumbing fix: `wiki_lint.py` made portable

`WIKI = Path("/Users/claudiobarone/Desktop/OSINT WORKSPACE/wiki")` was hardcoded — would have broken in CI on push #1. Replaced with `Path(os.environ.get("WIKI_DIR", Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "wiki"))` — auto-resolves from the script's own location, with `WIKI_DIR` env-var override for non-standard layouts. Verified working from arbitrary `cwd`.

### IP-redaction sweep (pre-hook prep)

Two tracked files contained the literal Hetzner IP and would have been false-positive blocked by the new hook on subsequent edits:
- **`ROADMAP.md` line 55** (dev-infra table documenting the hook itself) — replaced literal IP with `<server-ip>`.
- **`scripts/phase2_spawn.py` line 280** (legacy entity-spawn narrative) — replaced `root@<literal-ip>` with `root@<see .local/server.md>`, matching the pattern from the prior 2026-04-24 IP-scrub on `CLAUDE.md` + `wiki/entities/infrastructure/hetzner.md`.
- **`wiki/log.md` line 217** (historical log entry documenting the prior scrub) — left intact. The hook scans only added lines, so this never trips on routine log appends near the top of the file.

### Hook validation (4-pattern test matrix)

| Test | Input | Expected | Got |
|---|---|---|---|
| Positive | Stage the legitimate B′ files | exit 0 | ✅ exit 0 |
| Server IP | `_leak_test.md` containing `ssh root@<literal-ip>` | block | ✅ blocked |
| API key | `_leak_test.md` containing a 28-char `sk-…` token | block | ✅ blocked |
| `.env*` filename | `git add -f .env.test` | block | ✅ blocked |
| `.DS_Store` filename | `git add -f .DS_Store` | block | ✅ blocked |

All test artifacts cleaned up; staged tree returned to just the legitimate B′ changes before commit.

**Self-meta validation**: the hook tripped a sixth time — on the first attempt to commit *this very entry*, because the API-key test-matrix row originally contained the literal `sk-…<28 chars>` token used in the negative test. Redacted to "28-char `sk-…` token" and re-committed. Confirms the hook's content-not-intent stance: it doesn't care whether you're documenting the leak or causing it.

## [2026-04-25] lint+ingest | B loop-closers: SSRN 3000747 resolved + deepseek-api dates added

Two mechanical follow-ups from prior sessions closed.

### B.1 — SSRN 3000747 provenance resolved via local PDF extraction

`wiki/sources/stock-7-4-5.md` was a stub with `[verification pending]` on its provenance — the SSRN abstract page is not crawlable via Exa (`SOURCE_NOT_AVAILABLE` on both URL forms), and a prior `web_search_exa` pass didn't surface the specific abstract. Resolved by `pypdf` extraction of the local PDF.

**Confirmed identity:**
- **Title**: The Influence of Financial Distress Using Altman Z-Score, The Beta of Stocks and Inflation To The Stock Return
- **Authors**: Mathius Tandiontong and Margaretha Sitompul (Maranatha Christian University, Bandung, Indonesia)
- **Journal**: Journal of Finance and Banking Review (GATR Journals), ISSN 0128-3103, Vol. 2(2): 21–27, 2017
- **Content**: Empirical panel regression (75 Indonesian manufacturing companies, BEI 2008–2012). All three hypotheses confirmed: Altman Z-Score, beta stocks, and inflation each partially affect stock returns. R² = 28.7%.

Prior diction note ("Stock is one securities among other securities") was correctly flagged as regional/non-native-English — this is an Indonesian institutional paper, not a quality issue. Cluster updated `uncategorized → quant-finance`. `maturity: draft → validated`. Three verbatim snippets populated. `wiki/index.md` title entry replaced — removed the `⚠ SSRN page not crawlable` placeholder with real title + provenance metadata.

### B.2 — Undated `[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` tags in `deepseek-api.md` dated

Lint section 7 surfaced 3 undated `[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` tags (lines 39, 48, 62) in `wiki/entities/infrastructure/deepseek-api.md`. | `sources/stock-6-4-5.md` | SSRN 2654872 = *Comparable Stocks, Boundedly Rational Stock Markets and IPO Entry Rates* (Chok+Qian, Sept 2015, PLoS ONE 8(5):e61474) | `[CONFIRMED]` |

### Reminder for next ingest

Filename convention is **`<TICKER> <date>.docx`** — leading-underscore-and-parens forms (`_(ADA)3_24.docx`) get skipped by the regex. The script's diagnostic message ("SKIP unknown filename pattern") is clear enough that rename-and-rerun is a 2-step fix, but worth surfacing the rule each time the watchlist gets touched.

## [2026-04-25] capability | Exa MCP wired in — external research now first-class

The wiki gains an external-research tool path. Until today, "search the
wiki first, raw sources second, external sources last" had no defined
external-source mechanism — `WebFetch` / `WebSearch` were available but
not part of the schema. Now they are, via Exa MCP, with `crawling_exa`
giving us LLM-clean primary-source text on demand.

### What was actually done

1. **Plugin install** — `claude plugin marketplace update claude-plugins-official`
   then `claude plugin i exa@claude-plugins-official` (v3.3.8, scope: user,
   status: enabled). Provides the `exa:search` orchestrator skill.
2. **MCP server registration via API key** — the plugin's MCP server
   stayed stuck in `mcp-needs-auth-cache.json` (OAuth flow not completed).
   Bypassed via `claude mcp add --transport http exa "https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp?exaApiKey=...&tools=web_search_exa,web_search_advanced_exa,get_code_context_exa,crawling_exa,company_research_exa,people_search_exa,deep_researcher_start,deep_researcher_check"`.
   Registration landed in `~/.claude/.claude.json` user-scope, project-
   pinned to this workspace path. After Claude Code restart, all 8
   `mcp__exa__*` tools surfaced in the deferred-tool registry. The
   plugin's OAuth-required server still appears in `claude mcp list` as
   `plugin:exa:exa` (status: needs auth) — harmless duplicate listing.
3. **Smoke test** — `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` query on "CFTC Enforcement
   Advisory issued February 25 2026 regarding material non-public
   information MNPI" returned the **primary-source URL**
   `https://www.cftc.gov/media/13351/Enf_AdvisoryKalshi022526/download` for
   the exact advisory cited in `sources/3-19-compliance-research.md`.
   The wiki had the date and topic but not the document URL — Exa
   discovered it on query #1. Candidate follow-up: `crawling_exa` that
   URL into the compliance source's `## Snippets` to convert the
   currently-paraphrased CFTC claim into verifiable text.
4. **`CLAUDE.md` schema update** — new section `## External research —
   Exa MCP` inserted between Lint and Distribution rules. Maps each of
   the 8 tools to its workflow role, then describes integration into
   each operation (Ingest / Query OOD signal / Lint NEEDS-VERIFICATION
   tag resolution). Cost-discipline note: default `numResults: 3–5`,
   reserve `deep_researcher_*` for legitimate multi-source synthesis.
5. **Working-method rule update** — "External sources last" amended to
   "External sources last (via Exa MCP — see External research section)"
   so the rule now points to the mechanism.
6. **Path correction** — `/tmp/index_tickers.py` reference in the per-
   ticker research thread updated to `scripts/index_tickers.py`
   (script was relocated yesterday).

### Why this matters

Three workflow upgrades, in increasing order of leverage:

1. **URL-cited claims become verifiable**. Today many `## Snippets`
   sections cite URLs but contain only paraphrased text. With
   `crawling_exa` we can populate verbatim primary-source text for any
   URL-cited claim at the cost of one MCP call.
2. **OOD answers stop being terminal**. Previously, when a query hit a
   wiki gap, the response was "we don't have that, want to ingest?"
   Now the response can be "we don't have that — Exa returned X, here's
   the synthesis, want me to file it as a new source page?"
3. **`[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` tags become resolvable in-loop**. Lint
   passes can now upgrade or retract claims live, rather than queueing
   them as future work.

### Lint state delta

| Metric | Before | After | Δ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tracked schema operations | 3 (Ingest / Query / Lint) | 4 (+ External research) | +1 |
| Available MCP tools (Exa) | 0 | 8 | +8 |
| Workflow rules referencing Exa | 0 | 4 (Ingest / Query / Lint / Working method) | +4 |

### Candidate follow-ups (deferred)

- `crawling_exa` the CFTC Kalshi MNPI advisory URL into
  `sources/3-19-compliance-research.md` `## Snippets` and bump the
  cite from paraphrase to verbatim.
- Audit `wiki/sources/*.md` for URL-cited claims with no corresponding
  `## Snippets` entries; queue a batch `crawling_exa` pass.
- Extend `scripts/wiki_lint.py` to flag `[NEEDS VERIFICATION]` claims
  older than N days as Exa-resolution candidates.

## [2026-04-25] structural | git scaffold — workspace now version-controlled

User approved turning the workspace into a private GitHub repo for backup
and versioning. Initial commit `d2564c0` ("Initial commit: wiki, scripts,
schema baseline"). The push to a remote is the user's next step; this
log entry covers the local scaffold only.

1. **`scripts/`** — created at workspace root; copied 11 Python helpers
   from `/tmp/` into it (`bootstrap_extract.py`, `extract_docx.py`,
   `generate_stubs.py`, `index_tickers.py`, `partition_phase1.py`,
   `phase2_aggregate.py`, `phase2_enrich_docx.py`, `phase2_spawn.py`,
   `phase3_lint.py`, `phase3_lint_fix.py`, `wiki_lint.py`). They were
   ephemeral in `/tmp/` and would have been lost on next macOS reboot;
   now durable.
2. **`.local/`** — created at workspace root; populated with `server.md`
   holding the Hetzner SSH target, server root path, allowed/disallowed
   operations from this workspace. Gitignored — never committed.
3. **Server-IP scrub** — replaced inline `5.161.53.103` with pointer-to-
   `.local/server.md` in three tracked files:
   - `CLAUDE.md` line 222 ("Related — read-only" section)
   - `wiki/entities/infrastructure/hetzner.md` line 40 (server-reference bullet)
   - `wiki/log.md` line 390 (brief-transfer scp entry)
   Remaining IP occurrences in `briefs/*.md` (5 files) and
   `.claude/settings.local.json` are gitignored, so untouched.
4. **`.gitignore`** — created. Excludes raw sources (`research for cemini/`,
   `market dataset/`, `research to be indexed/`), operational outputs
   (`briefs/`), credentials (`.local/`, `.claude/settings.local.json`),
   OS/editor cruft (`.DS_Store`, `__pycache__/`, etc.).
5. **`README.md`** — created at workspace root. One-page orientation:
   what's tracked, what's not (and why), three-layer architecture
   reminder, distribution targets, getting-started snippet.
6. **`git init` + initial commit** — 325 files staged, all under
   `CLAUDE.md`, `LESSONS.md`, `README.md`, `wiki/`, `scripts/`, and
   `.gitignore`. Verified the gitignored paths (briefs, .local, .claude,
   research for cemini, market dataset, research to be indexed) are
   absent from `git ls-files --cached`.

### Decisions worth remembering

- **`briefs/` is gitignored** (revised from earlier plan to commit).
  Briefs are operational/throwaway and contained five inline server-IP
  references. The wiki + `log.md` entries already document everything
  durable about each brief; the brief files themselves are point-in-time
  artifacts.
- **`.claude/settings.local.json` is gitignored** by the established
  `.local.json` Claude Code convention. Contained 8+ allow-listed bash
  commands embedding the server IP.
- **No remote configured.** User creates the private GitHub repo at
  their leisure and runs `git remote add origin … && git push -u origin
  main` themselves.

### Lint state delta

| version-controlled | no | yes (local) |
| inline server-IP occurrences in tracked files | 8 | 0 |
| Python scripts surviving `/tmp/` cleanup | 0 | 11 (in `scripts/`) |

## [2026-04-24] milestone | Server-triage brief closeout + equity-intraday data-gap discovered + fix shipped

Server-Claude data audit returned three streams of results — all closing briefs cleanly with one new operational finding.

### Workstream A (Ollama hunt) ✅ clean
- **Zero consumer-path hits** for `ollama` / `OLLAMA` / `11434` across `agents/`, `services/`, `scripts/`, `docker/`, `.env`.
- All remaining references are **cosmetic wiki docs only** (retraction banners + historical notes) — no runtime impact.
- Root cause of user's "still looking for Ollama" symptom re-framed: was surfacing through `wiki_search` reads of retraction-banner text in `/opt/cemini/research_wiki/` pages, not from a live runtime reach. Cosmetic.
- **No rewire/remove PRs needed.** Brief closed.

### Workstream B (research_wiki refresh) ✅ complete — with divergence note
- **12 ticker pages added to server's `research_wiki/` via DeepSeek generation** (5 frontier: INTC/GLW/PL/ASTS/SOFI + 7 fills: COIN/DIS/MSTR/NFLX/ETH/SOL/DOGE). Server now carries 24 ticker pages + 304 total wiki pages.
- **UBER orphan reaped** from the DB, audit-chain entry written. This exercises the `--prune` path that was parked at end-of-Track-B-rebuild. One full prune round-trip complete.
- **Divergence flag**: the 12 server pages were **generated fresh via DeepSeek**, not rsync-copied from workspace. Workspace still holds the Gemini Deep Research bodies (institutional template, 6-section format). Server's `wiki_search` now embeds DeepSeek-generated bodies. Net: workspace = human-curated librarian (Gemini DR source-of-truth); server `research_wiki/` = RAG-lookup cache for LangGraph analyst (DeepSeek-regenerated). Same slug namespace, different body methodology. **Not reconciling automatically** — the two artifacts serve different consumers (workspace→user; research_wiki→LLM). Revisit if/when Gemini DR bodies become materially more accurate than DeepSeek regeneration for a given ticker.

### Data-audit result — new operational finding (equity intraday gap)

- **Crypto (Coinbase)** ✅ healthy all day — BTC/ETH/SOL etc. live through 21:10 UTC.
- **Equities** ❌ **missed full market session 13:30–20:00 UTC 2026-04-24**. Root cause: Polygon plan returns `NOT_AUTHORIZED` for same-day intraday data on equity ticks; `polygon_ingestor` market-hours gate silently skipped post-close once the NOT_AUTHORIZED came back. New tickers (INTC/GLW/PL/ASTS/SOFI) were backfilled from Apr 22 historical at container startup, so they had Apr 23 coverage but not Apr 24 live. GLW notably thinner (882 ticks) vs INTC/SOFI (~1,500+) — volume/liquidity tell.
- **Fix 1 (point fix)** — `scripts/backfill_yfinance.py` ran and inserted **13,258 bars** (390 × 34) for Apr 24's full session. All 5 new tickers verified: INTC $82.56, GLW $175.92, PL $35.43, ASTS $76.42, SOFI $18.45.
- **Fix 2 (doctrine)** — `polygon_ingestor.py` patched to add a **yfinance EOD sweep**: after 16:01 ET daily, ingestor checks if the session is missing and auto-backfills via yfinance. `yfinance` added to Dockerfile. Ingestor is now self-healing on the Polygon-intraday-NOT_AUTHORIZED failure mode going forward.

## [2026-04-30] deep-read | Polymarket Informed Minority (Gómez-Cram et al., SSRN 6617059)

Promoted from `read-skim` → `deep-read`. Triangulated across 8 independent secondary sources (CoinDesk, TradingView/Cointelegraph, Cryptonews, Bitcoin News, Crowdfund Insider, Verse Press, iGaming.org, MEXC News) plus the SSRN abstract — highest-fidelity read achievable without SSRN browser session. SSRN blocks programmatic crawling; primary PDF still not downloaded.

### What changed
- `sources/polymarket-informed-minority-gomezcram-2026.md` — major rewrite. Added: Phase 3 Signal-Detection Criteria section with 6 rule families (sign-randomization test, order-flow informativeness, news-reaction test, insider-trading anomaly detection, persistence validation, concentration monitoring) encoding concrete thresholds for the dual-MCP-Polymarket brief's shadow-mode design. New quantitative parameters: 12% of raw-profit top-earners beat the randomization benchmark; 60% of lucky winners revert to losers out-of-sample; 1,950 accounts flagged for insider-trading patterns ($15K avg profit); insider trades move prices 7-12× more per dollar; news-reaction behavior as the cleanest skill/not-skill discriminator; on-chain concentration data (top 0.04% wallets = 70% of profits). 11 new verbatim snippets with source attribution.

### Key signal-detection parameters surfaced
1. Sign-randomization: 10,000 iterations, p<0.05 threshold, minimum ~20-30 trades per account
2. Order-flow: 1pp skilled net buying → 8bp P(correct outcome); lucky-winner flow gets zero weight
3. News-reaction: only skilled traders adjust around FOMC/earnings — the cleanest single discriminator
4. Insider flags: new-account + large-position + <15% odds, dormancy-after-win, price-impact >7× baseline
5. Persistence: weekly reclassification, 44% expected retention, anti-skill fade signal (51% persistent)
6. Concentration: dashboard monitoring, not signal-generating

## [2026-04-30] deep-read | agriciDaniel/claude-obsidian — /autoresearch loop, hooks.json, wiki-lint

Promoted from `read-skim` → `deep-read`. Full crawl of README.md, WIKI.md (13-section schema reference), hooks.json (4 lifecycle events), commands/autoresearch.md, skills/autoresearch/references/program.md, agents/wiki-lint.md via raw.githubusercontent.com.

### Three directly portable extracts for Cemini Tier-2.5

1. **`/autoresearch` loop control structure** — 3 rounds max, 15 pages/session cap, 5 sources/round, confidence scoring (high/medium/low), output discipline (declarative, no hedging, cite everything). Complete specification for porting the manual Tier-2 sweep into `scripts/tier25_sweep.py` with `tier25_sweep_config.yaml`.

2. **`hooks.json` session-state architecture** — SessionStart (silently read hot.md), PostCompact (re-read hot.md after compaction), PostToolUse (auto-commit on Write/Edit), Stop (update hot.md if wiki changed). The SessionStart/Stop pair directly extends the cpr-pattern the ROADMAP references.

## [2026-05-02] ingest | Recursive Multi-Agent Systems (arXiv:2604.25917)

- Created `wiki/sources/recursive-multi-agent-systems-2604.25917.md` — deep-read, 14 snippet pulls

Key findings: 2-layer residual RecursiveLink replaces text-based agent communication. Only 0.31% of params trained. +8.3% accuracy, 1.2×–2.4× speedup, 34.6%–75.6% token reduction across 9 benchmarks. Inner-outer loop training enables gradient-based credit assignment across pipeline stages. Direct relevance to Conductor Path C.

## [2026-05-08] phase0-audit | hftbacktest (K31) — MIT, Production/Stable ✅

Phase 0 source audit for `@wiki/entities/tools/hftbacktest.md` per K31 Gemini DR eval URL 15 (Adopt tier).

### Audit outcomes

- **License**: MIT confirmed — `LICENSE` file present in repo root, standard MIT text with "Permission is hereby granted" preamble. Matches eval claim. **[CONFIRMED]**
- **Maturity**: PyPI maturity **Production/Stable (5/5)**. Latest: `rust-v0.9.4` / `py-v2.4.4` (Dec 10, 2025). 4,015 ★, 780 forks, 30 contributors. Last commit Dec 23, 2025 (active within 90 days).
- **Rust toolchain**: Pre-built bdist_wheel on PyPI includes compiled Rust backend. **No Rust toolchain needed on Hetzner** — `pip install hftbacktest` succeeded in venv on `cemini-librarian`. Resolves Risk #13 from brief.
- **Build system**: `maturin` build backend; Rust >= 1.91.1 only required if building from source.
- **Data format compatibility**: Ingests Level-2 MBP + Level-3 MBO tick-by-tick data. **Venue-agnostic** — not tied to equity exchanges. Polymarket CLOB and Kalshi orderbook can be adapted via shim layers (adapter risk remains for Phase 1).
- **Polars compatibility**: First-class `polars` dependency — aligns with Cemini's existing Polars-based data pipeline.
- **Compute footprint**: Depends on `numba` (JIT) + `polars` (columnar) — memory profile acceptable for 6-month windows on Hetzner CPX31 (7 GB) for single-venue backtests; multi-venue × 100+ contracts needs Phase 1 validation.
- **No dead code or license traps found.**

### Verdict: **GO** ✅

License clear (MIT), maturity high (Production/Stable), no toolchain blockers. Proceed to Phase 1 sandbox replication per brief.

### Pages updated
- `wiki/entities/tools/hftbacktest.md` — maturity raised `draft` → `validated`, added verified fields (repo URL, license, release, stack, Rust note), updated open questions, added install snippet
- `wiki/index.md` — maturity tag `DRAFT 2026-05-05` → `VALIDATED 2026-05-08`

### Librarian installs (cemini-librarian)
- **turbovec** — rustup toolchain installed; built after `apt install libssl-dev pkg-config libopenblas-dev`; Python bindings via maturin into `/opt/turbovec-venv` (needed a `patchelf --add-needed libopenblas.so` fix for an undefined `cblas_sgemm`).
- **LEANN** — `/opt/leann-venv` on Python 3.12 (no version pins needed, unlike the laptop's 3.9). 5.6 GB venv.

### Benchmark vs kb-server's brute-force search (943-vector live corpus)
kb-server (`/opt/kb-server`, Node) embeds with all-MiniLM-L6-v2, stores in SQLite, searches brute-force; its own `search.js` says brute-force is fine to <2000 notes. Corpus is **943 vectors** (one embedding/doc, no chunking).
- brute-force: 23 µs/q, recall 1.000 (exact).
- turbovec bw=4/3/2: 16/19/25 µs/q, recall@10 0.92/0.88/0.81, index 180/136/92 KB.
- LEANN: 19 min 23 s build, 1.5 GB RAM peak, search unstable (core-dumped / empty) on the 3.7 GB host.

### Decision — NO-GO on wiring (new concept page)
- `wiki/concepts/kb-server-retrieval-scaling.md` (validated) — at 943 vectors brute-force is optimal; ANN indexes pay off at 10⁵–10⁷ vectors. turbovec/LEANN are Python/Rust vs kb-server's Node → sidecar mismatch. Trigger to revisit: corpus > ~2000 chunks → evaluate `sqlite-vss` (in-Node, in-SQLite) first.
- kb-server left untouched, verified healthy (HTTP 200) before and after.
- turbovec + LEANN remain staged on the librarian for the day the corpus is genuinely large.
- Entity pages `turbovec.md` + `leann.md` updated with librarian-deployment sections + benchmark; backlinks on `conductor-librarian-architecture.md` and the K47 source page.

## [2026-05-17] infra | wiki-sync cron → git post-commit hook (macOS TCC fix)

The hourly `sync_wiki_to_librarian.sh` cron had silently never run — the librarian's wiki copy went 5 days stale (2026-05-12 → 2026-05-17). Root cause: the workspace lives under `~/Desktop`, a macOS TCC-protected location; cron- and launchd-scheduled background jobs cannot read it without Full Disk Access. Confirmed: all 3 cron jobs (wiki-sync, librarian-healthcheck, prod-healthcheck) had zero scheduled runs ever; a launchd-agent test reproduced it exactly (`Operation not permitted`, exit 126).

Fix: replaced the time-scheduled sync with a **git `post-commit` hook** (`scripts/post-commit.sh`, installed via `install-hooks.sh`). The hook runs inside the interactive session — which already has the necessary file access — and fires the sync, detached, whenever a commit touches `wiki/`. The librarian now refreshes exactly when the wiki changes rather than on a fixed hour. The dead `0 * * * *` sync cron line was removed; the TCC-blocked launchd agent was removed.

Still dead from the same TCC cause (not commit-triggered, so not fixed by the hook): the 5-min `librarian_healthcheck` and daily `cemini_prod_healthcheck` crons — these need a Full Disk Access grant to run scheduled, flagged for follow-up.

## [2026-05-17] infra | cron Full Disk Access granted — healthchecks live, hourly sync restored as backstop

Followup to the TCC diagnosis: `/usr/sbin/cron` was granted Full Disk Access, so cron can now read `~/Desktop`. Consequences:
- The two cron jobs that were silently dead — 5-min `librarian_healthcheck`, daily `cemini_prod_healthcheck` — now run.
- Restored the hourly `sync_wiki_to_librarian.sh` cron as a **backstop**: the git post-commit hook stays the primary sync trigger (instant, on every wiki commit), and the hourly cron now catches the gaps the hook can't see — sibling-wiki commits made in their own repos, and any uncommitted drift.
- `sync_wiki_to_librarian.sh` gained an atomic `mkdir` single-run lock (stale-lock reclaim >15 min) so the two triggers can't pile up overlapping runs.

## [2026-05-18] new pages | API Credential Registry + Non-Latency Arbitrage Strategies

Operator request: track API keys in a wiki so the bot can find a key it needs; track the arb strategies just added to the bot, focusing on non-latency-sensitive ones.

### Declined as requested, redirected
The request also asked to catalog third-party repos of "thousands of free keys" so the bot could follow source → repo → key and *use* the keys. Declined: those are credential dumps belonging to other accounts; using them is unauthorized access / theft of service, exposes Cemini IPs to honeypot keys, and fights the repo's `detect-secrets` hook. Redirected to a legitimate credential *inventory* instead. The decision is documented in-page (Raw Concept + Dead Ends of `api-credential-registry.md`).

### New pages
- `concepts/api-credential-registry.md` — credential **inventory**, not a value store. Per-API: purpose / free-tier limits / official signup URL / key held? / where the secret lives. Never records a key value. Skeleton seeded with ~30 APIs across market data, crypto, economic, news, weather, LLM, OSINT. Bot-usage chain: query → this page → read the real secret from runtime env. Missing key → Signup URL column.
- `concepts/non-latency-arbitrage-strategies.md` — four standing-order arb strategies: YES/NO complement, Dutch-book / sum-of-outcomes, cross-venue on slow/long-dated contracts, calendar/resolution-date (monotonicity). Edge is a logical inconsistency, not a ms-scale quote race. Universal cost gate + annualize-the-locked-return discipline. Slow-arb complement to `cross-venue-arbitrage-pattern.md`.

### Backlinks added (bidirectional)
- `entities/tools/public-apis-list.md` ↔ `api-credential-registry.md` (discovery directory ↔ committed inventory)
- `concepts/cross-venue-arbitrage-pattern.md` ↔ `non-latency-arbitrage-strategies.md` (fast vs slow arb)
- `concepts/favorite-longshot-bias.md` ↔ `non-latency-arbitrage-strategies.md` (FLB mispricing feeds standing arb)

`index.md` updated (+2 Concept rows). `updated:` bumped on the 3 backlinked pages.

### Update — registry populated from `.env` (2026-05-18)

Operator confirmed keys live in `.env`. Extracted variable *names only* (never values) from `cemini-prod:/opt/cemini/.env` (28 API keys) + laptop workspace `.env` (DeepSeek/Gemini/Grok/Librarian). Registry `Key?` and `Stored at` columns populated against real variable names. New rows added for keys held but not previously listed: Coinbase, Robinhood, Google BigQuery, GNews, NewsAPI, X/Twitter API, OpenRouter, Gemini, Grok, plus a dev-token table (GitHub PAT, Cloudflare Tunnel, Librarian). Both `.env` files verified gitignored + untracked. No key value entered the wiki.

### Update — gbrain retired (2026-05-18)

Decommissioned gbrain (the vector + knowledge-graph search MCP). Adopted in K50 but never load-bearing: a third retrieval layer redundant with kb-server (full-text, what the trading bot uses) and direct file reads (always current). Its git-based sync also conflicted with the rsync wiki-sync, so keeping it current would have meant an ongoing maintenance cron. Removed from `.mcp.json` (`local` + `librarian` envs); entity page + index + ROADMAP updated. Install left inert on cemini-librarian (`/opt/gbrain` 328M, `/root/.gbrain` 277M) — purgeable later. kb-server verified current first: both new K-series pages (api-credential-registry, non-latency-arbitrage-strategies) indexed, so the bot lost nothing.

But Post 4 fabricated the L2 detail — the actual dataset is **trades + market metadata** (36 GiB compressed), not "L2 order-book snapshots, 20 levels, 100ms, 850M state updates," and every setup command in the post is wrong (`cd prediction-market-arbitrage` 404s; `make download-ob` / `orderbooks_l2.tar.zst` / `data/snapshots/` do not exist). New entity page `entities/tools/prediction-market-analysis.md` (Adopt-eligible, MIT, server-side); it consolidates a prior K30 `5_5-links-2` URL-6 Steal-from mention. Consequence recorded on `prediction-market-statistical-arbitrage.md`: Method 1 (cointegration/OU) is backtestable from the real dataset; Method 2 (OBI/micro-price) is not — `[RETRACTED]` the L2 claim, logged in that page's Dead Ends.

## [2026-05-19] K54 ingest | Polymarket Bridge API (@AlterEgo_eth X post) + 26-URL multi-wiki tool evaluation

Two inbox sources processed as one K-batch.

### Source 1 — @AlterEgo_eth Polymarket Bridge API (`Post.docx`)

A single X post documenting Polymarket's **Bridge API** (`bridge.polymarket.com`) — a sixth API surface beyond Gamma / Data / CLOB / LB-API / WebSockets, handling wallet *funding*. Five endpoints: `POST /deposit` (multi-chain EVM/SVM/BTC addresses), `GET /supported-assets`, `POST /quote`, `GET /status/{address}`, `POST /withdraw`. All deposits auto-convert to **pUSD** (Polymarket USD) on Polygon. The deposit flow must end with a CLOB `/balance-allowance/update` call — skipping it leaves the new balance invisible to the order layer (silent funding failure).

- New source page `sources/alterego-eth-polymarket-bridge-api.md` (validated, read).
- New entity stub `entities/tools/polymarket-bridge-api.md`.
- `concepts/polymarket-api-reference.md` updated five-surface → **six-surface**; new Bridge API section; title + frontmatter bumped.
- `entities/people/alteregoeth.md` updated — fourth ingested artifact from this handle.
- "No auth required" claim tagged `[verification pending]` — single-source, and this author's prior cheat sheet had a wrong auth column.

### Source 2 — 26-URL multi-wiki tool evaluation (`Tool Evaluation and Wiki Fit.docx`)

Fifth+ multi-wiki tool-eval batch in a month. Eval verdicts: 6 Adopt / 1 Steal-from / 5 Reference / 11 Reject / 3 UNAVAILABLE.

**Ingested with verification corrections — the eval's license layer was wrong on 9 of 26.** GitHub-API check (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>`, 2026-05-19) across all 23 retrievable repos found two failure modes: 8 false "NO LICENSE FOUND" *and* one fabricated license. Of 10 repos the eval marked no-license, only 2 are genuinely unlicensed (GitDorker, pro-workflow); the rest are MIT/Apache/AGPL/GPL. **MoneyPrinter was Rejected as a "catastrophic AGPL-3.0 poison pill" — it is verified MIT; the eval invented the AGPL.** Net flips: 3 Rejects overturned to license-clean (EverOS Apache-2.0, LLMFeeder MIT, poly-arbitrage-bot MIT — the last re-confirming K53); 3 genuine copyleft poison pills correctly rejected but for the wrong stated reason (TruffleHog AGPL-3.0, gitGraber GPL-3.0, H4X-Tools GPL-3.0); NextTrade's stack misidentified (TypeScript, not Python) and ~1yr stale.

#### New pages (4)

- `sources/multi-wiki-tool-eval-26url-2026-05-19.md` — source page (validated, deep-read) with full 26-URL verification table.

- `entities/tools/nexttrade.md` — Reference (eval Adopt downgraded); MIT, but TypeScript not Python and ~1yr stale.
- `entities/tools/yt-dlp.md` — Adopt (source-build only); Unlicense source, GPLv3+-encumbered binaries must not be vendored.

#### Updated (existing)

- `concepts/cemini-license-posture.md` — K54 addendum: eval license layer fails both directions; restated rule (re-verify *every* license claim, not just no-license Rejects).
